&\qquad\leq\int_{Q_{T}}\left(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2}\right)\varphi-\ell_{1}+2\ell_{2},\end{split}

(2.95)

where

\displaystyle\ell_{1}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{m\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{Q_{T}}\left(|\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}|^{2}G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{2})-\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}\mathbb{F}_{k}:\mathbb{F}\right)\varphi,

(2.96)

\displaystyle\ell_{2}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{m\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{Q_{T}}\left(\nabla\bm{v}_{k}:G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{2})\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}-\nabla\bm{v}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}:\mathbb{F}\right)\varphi.

(2.97)

Step 2.

We shall prove that
\ell_{1}\geq 0
and

\ell_{2}\leq\int_{Q_{T}}\tilde{L}\left(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2}\right)\varphi

(2.98)

for all non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}((-\infty,T)\times\Omega)
,
where
\tilde{L}
is an
L^{2}(Q_{T})
function specified below. This, together with the results from Step 1, implies the validity of (
2.93
), and thus completes the proof of the compactness of
\{\mathbb{F}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
in
(L^{2}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
, as clarified in the beginning of this subsection.

We concentrate just on the proof of (
2.98
) since the previous results can be derived following step by step the proofs of the corresponding properties in the isothermal setting, see
[
14
]
, Proof of the compactness of
\{\mathbb{F}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
in
(L^{2}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
, Steps 1a, 1b, 2a.

Proof of (
2.98
).
.

First we decompose
l_{2}
into three parts
L_{1}+L_{2}+L_{3},

where

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{m\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{Q_{T}}\left(\nabla\bm{v}_{k}:G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{2})\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}-\nabla\bm{v}:\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}\right)\varphi,

(2.99)

\displaystyle L_{2}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{Q_{T}}\left(\nabla\bm{v}:(\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}-\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T})\right)\varphi,

(2.100)

\displaystyle L_{3}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{Q_{T}}\left(\nabla\bm{v}\mathbb{F}:\mathbb{F}-\nabla\bm{v}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}:\mathbb{F}\right)\varphi.

(2.101)

For
L_{2}
, due to (
2.59
) and the fact that
\nabla\bm{v}\in(L^{2}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
, there holds

L_{2}\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla\bm{v}||\mathbb{F}_{k}-\mathbb{F}|^{2}\varphi=\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla\bm{v}|\left(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2}\right)\varphi.

(2.102)

To treat
L_{1}
and
L_{3}
, let us first decompose
\bm{v}_{k}:=\bm{v}_{k}^{1}+\bm{v}_{k}^{2}
, where
\bm{v}_{k}^{1}\in L^{2}(0,T;W_{\bm{n},\diver}^{1,2})
solves, for all
\bm{w}\in W_{\bm{n},\diver}^{1,2}
and almost all
t\in(0,T)
, the problem

\langle\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{k}^{1},\bm{w}\rangle+\int_{\Omega}\nabla\bm{v}_{k}^{1}:\nabla\bm{w}+\int_{\partial\Omega}\bm{v}_{k_{\tau}}^{1}\cdot\bm{w}_{\tau}=-\int_{\Omega}g(\theta_{k})\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}:\nabla\bm{w}

(2.103)

with the initial condition
\bm{v}_{k}^{1}(0,\bm{\cdot})=\bm{v}_{0}
almost everywhere in
\Omega
. Since
\bm{v}_{k}^{2}=\bm{v}_{k}-\bm{v}_{k}^{1}
, we know that
\bm{v}_{k}^{2}\in L^{2}(0,T;W_{\bm{n},\diver}^{1,2})
solves, for all
\bm{w}\in W_{\bm{n},\diver}^{1,2}
and a.a.
t\in(0,T)
, the problem

\langle\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{k}^{2},\bm{w}\rangle+\int_{\Omega}\nabla\bm{v}_{k}^{2}:\nabla\bm{w}+\int_{\partial\Omega}\bm{v}_{k_{\tau}}^{2}\cdot\bm{w}_{\tau}=\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}_{k}\otimes\bm{v}_{k}):\nabla\bm{w}

(2.104)

with the initial condition
\bm{v}_{k}^{2}(0,\bm{\cdot})=\bm{0}
almost everywhere in
\Omega
.
Since
\{\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded in
(L^{2}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
, the standard theory of Stokes problems applied on the equation (
2.103
) gives the existence of
\bm{v}^{1}
such that (for suitable not relabeled subsequence of
\{\bm{v}_{k}^{1}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
)

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{k}^{1}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\bm{v}^{1}

\displaystyle\mbox{weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W_{\bm{n},\diver}^{1,2}),

(2.105)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{k}^{1}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{1}

\displaystyle\mbox{weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;(W_{\bm{n},\diver}^{1,2})^{*}).

(2.106)

Due to (
2.58
), (
2.61
) and the Aubin-Lions compactness lemma, we have that

\bm{v}_{k}\to\bm{v}\quad\mbox{ strongly in }(L^{\frac{10}{3})}(Q_{T}))^{3},

(2.107)

thus the standard theory of Stokes problems (together with (
2.58
) and (
2.105
)) applied on the equation (
2.104
) gives the existence of
\bm{v}^{2}
satisfying (for suitable not relabeled subsequence of
\{\bm{v}_{k}^{2}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
)

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\bm{v}^{2}

\displaystyle\mbox{weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W_{\bm{n},\diver}^{1,2}),

(2.108)

\displaystyle\nabla\bm{v}_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\to\nabla\bm{v}^{2}

\displaystyle\mbox{strongly in }(L^{2)}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}.

(2.109)

We shall deal with biting limits of terms involving
\nabla\bm{v}^{1}_{k}
. We define the sequence
\{b_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
as

b_{k}:=|\nabla\bm{v}_{k}^{1}|^{2}+|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{4}.

(2.110)

Thanks to (
2.17
) and (
2.105
), the sequence
\{b_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{1}(Q_{T})
.
Hence, Chacon’s biting lemma (Proposition
1.2
) and the characterisation of
L^{1}
-weakly converging sequences imply, for a suitable not relabeled subsequence of
\{b_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, the existence of a nondecreasing sequence of measurable sets
\{E_{j}\subset Q_{T}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
with the property
|Q_{T}\setminus E_{j}|\to 0
as
j\to\infty
and satisfying that, for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
and any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\tilde{\delta}>0
such that, for any measurable set
U\subset E_{j}
with
|U|<\tilde{\delta}
, there holds

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{U}b_{k}\leq\varepsilon.

(2.111)

Now we decompose the terms
L_{1}
and
L_{3}
in the following way:

L_{1}=L_{1}^{1}+L_{1}^{2},\qquad L_{3}=L_{3}^{1}+L_{3}^{2},

(2.112)

where

\displaystyle L_{1}^{1}:=\lim_{m\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}}\left(\nabla\bm{v}^{1}_{k}:G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{2})\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}-\nabla\bm{v}^{1}:\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}\right)\varphi,

\displaystyle L_{3}^{1}:=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}}\left(\nabla\bm{v}^{1}\mathbb{F}:\mathbb{F}-\nabla\bm{v}^{1}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}:\mathbb{F}\right)\varphi.

We start with a suitable identification of
L_{1}^{1}
. We first realize that (
C
depends on the data)

\left|\{G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{2})\neq 1\}\right|\leq\left|\{|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{2}\geq m\}\right|\leq\int_{\{|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{4}\geq m^{2}\}}\frac{|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{4}}{m^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{m^{2}},

(2.113)

which, due to the uniform equi-integrability of
\{b_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
on
E_{j}
(see (
2.111
)), the Young inequality and the fact that
G_{m}
is bounded, enables to rewrite
L_{1}^{1}
as

L_{1}^{1}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}}(\nabla\bm{v}_{k}^{1}-\nabla\bm{v}^{1}):\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}\varphi.

(2.114)

From the uniform equi-integrability of
\{b_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
on
E_{j}
and the boundedness of
g
it also follows that the sequence

\{\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{k}:\nabla\bm{v}_{k}^{1}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, where

\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{k}:=g(\theta_{k})\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}+\mathbb{D}_{k}^{1},

is uniformly equi-integrable on
E_{j}
as well. Hence, due to the properties of
E_{j}
, the convergence results (
2.105
) and (
2.106
), the weak convergence of (a subsequence of)
\{\mathbb{G}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
in
(L^{2}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
, the equation (
2.103
) and Proposition
1.3
, we have, for an appropiate not relabeled subsequence, that

\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{k}:\nabla\bm{v}_{k}^{1}\rightharpoonup\tilde{\mathbb{G}}:\nabla\bm{v}^{1}\quad\mbox{ weakly in }L^{1}(E_{j}),

(2.115)

and thus, for all
\psi\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
(and a further not relabeled subsequence), there holds

\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}}(\nabla\bm{v}_{k}^{1}-\nabla\bm{v}^{1}):g(\theta_{k})\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}\psi=-\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}}|\mathbb{D}_{k}^{1}-\mathbb{D}^{1}|^{2}\psi.

(2.116)

Next, since
\theta_{k}\to\theta
almost everywhere in
Q_{T}
and
\{\ln\theta_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{1}(Q_{T})
, which follows from (
2.19
) (with
\theta_{k}
in the role of
\theta
), by the Fatou lemma it holds that (recall that
\delta
is defined in (
1.19
), use the convention
\ln 0=-\infty
)

\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{\Omega}|\ln\theta(t)|\leq\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\liminf_{k\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}|\ln\theta_{k}(t)|\leq\tilde{C}(\delta,T,\Omega,g,\bm{v}_{0},\mathbb{B}_{0},\theta_{0}),

(2.117)

which directly gives that
\theta>0
almost everywhere in
Q_{T}
and that
\ln\theta\in L^{1}(Q_{T})
. Recall (from the footnote on page 9) that
g(\theta)>0
whenever
\theta>0
. Hence, due to the continuity of
g
, we have

\frac{1}{g(\theta_{k})}\to\frac{1}{g(\theta)}\quad\mbox{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

(2.118)

Moreover, thanks to the property that if
g(\theta)\to 0+
as
\theta\to 0+
, then
g(\theta)/\theta\to g^{\prime}(0)>0
(see again the footnote on page 9 and recall that
g
is nondecreasing, continuous and bounded), one can deduce, for any
M\in(0,\infty)
and some finite positive
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(g,M)
, that (in the first inequality we use again the Fatou lemma)

\begin{split}&\int_{Q_{T}}|\ln g(\theta)|\leq\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{Q_{T}}|\ln g(\theta_{k})|=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{\{\theta_{k}\leq M\}}|\ln g(\theta_{k})|+\int_{\{\theta_{k}>M\}}|\ln g(\theta_{k})|\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{\{\theta_{k}\leq M\}}\left|\ln\left(\frac{g(\theta_{k})}{\theta_{k}}\right)+\ln\theta_{k}\right|+|\{\theta_{k}>M\}|(|\ln g(M)|+|\ln g_{\infty}|)\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\left\{\begin{matrix}\begin{split}\sup_{k\in N}\int_{\{\theta_{k}\leq M\}}|\ln\theta_{k}|+|\{\theta_{k}\leq M\}|(|\ln g^{\prime}(0)|+\varepsilon(g,M))\\
\hskip 9.24994pt+|\{\theta_{k}>M\}|(|\ln g(M)|+|\ln g_{\infty}|)\end{split}&\mbox{ if }g(0)=0\\
\ \ \\
|Q_{T}|(|\ln g(0)|+|\ln g_{\infty}|+|\ln g(M)|)&\mbox{ if }g(0)>0\end{matrix}\right.,\end{split}

which, thanks to (
2.19
) and the properties of
g
, implies that
\ln g(\theta)\in L^{1}(Q_{T})
and
\{\ln g(\theta_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{1}(Q_{T})
.
Due to these facts we see that
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}|\{g(\theta_{k})\leq\frac{1}{l}\}|\to 0
and
|\{g(\theta)\leq\frac{1}{l}\}|\to 0
as
l\to\infty
.
Hence, the Egoroff theorem applied on (
2.118
) then implies that there exists a non-decreasing sequence of measurable sets
\{\mathcal{E}_{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
|Q_{T}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{l}|\to 0
as
l\to\infty
and

\frac{1}{g(\theta_{k})}\to\frac{1}{g(\theta)}\quad\mbox{ strongly in }L^{\infty}(\mathcal{E}_{l}).

(2.119)

Combining (
2.116
) and (
2.119
), we obtain, for all
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}((-\infty,T)\times\Omega)
, that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{l}}(\nabla\bm{v}_{k}^{1}-\nabla\bm{v}^{1}):\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}\varphi=-\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{l}}\frac{|\mathbb{D}^{1}_{k}-\mathbb{D}^{1}|^{2}}{g(\theta)}\varphi.

(2.120)

Hence, due to the uniform equi-integrability of the integrand on the left-hand side of (
2.120
) and also of
|\mathbb{D}_{k}^{1}-\mathbb{D}^{1}|^{2}
on
E_{j}
(see (
2.110
) and (
2.111
)), (
2.114
), and the properties of
\mathcal{E}_{l}
, we have, for all non-negative
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}((-\infty,T)\times\Omega)
, that

\begin{split}&L_{1}^{1}=\lim_{l\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{l}}(\nabla\bm{v}_{k}^{1}-\nabla\bm{v}^{1}):\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}\varphi\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\leq-\lim_{l\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{l}}\frac{|\mathbb{D}^{1}_{k}-\mathbb{D}^{1}|^{2}\varphi}{R}=-\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}}\frac{|\mathbb{D}^{1}_{k}-\mathbb{D}^{1}|^{2}\varphi}{R},\end{split}

(2.121)

where
R:=\sup_{\theta>0}\{g(\theta)\}\in(0,\infty)
.

Next, we introduce an appropriate estimate of
L_{3}^{1}
. Using (
2.105
), (
2.59
), Young’s inequality and the localised form of Korn’s inequality (see
[
13
]
, Step 2 of the proof of the compactness of
\{\mathbb{F}_{\varepsilon}\}
), we have that

\displaystyle L_{3}^{1}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}}\left((\nabla\bm{v}^{1}-\nabla\bm{v}^{1}_{k})(\mathbb{F}_{k}-\mathbb{F}):\mathbb{F}\right)\varphi

(2.122)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{R}|\mathbb{D}^{1}-\mathbb{D}^{1}_{k}|^{2}+\frac{R}{2}|\mathbb{F}_{k}-\mathbb{F}|^{2}|\mathbb{F}|^{2}\right)\varphi

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{E_{j}}\frac{1}{R}|\mathbb{D}^{1}-\mathbb{D}^{1}_{k}|^{2}\varphi+\int_{Q_{T}}\frac{R}{2}|\mathbb{F}|^{2}(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\varphi.

Summing (
2.121
) with (
2.122
), we obtain that

L_{1}^{1}+L_{3}^{1}\leq\int_{Q_{T}}\frac{R}{2}|\mathbb{F}|^{2}(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\varphi

(2.123)

with
R:=\sup_{\theta>0}\{g(\theta)\}
.

Last, we briefly show that

\lim_{j\to\infty}L_{1}^{2}=\lim_{j\to\infty}L_{3}^{2}=0.

(2.124)

From (
2.99
) and (
2.112
) it follows that

\begin{split}L_{1}^{2}=\lim_{m\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{Q_{T}\setminus E_{j}}\left(\nabla\bm{v}^{1}_{k}:G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{2})\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}-\nabla\bm{v}^{1}:\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}\right)\varphi\\
+\int_{Q_{T}}\left(\nabla\bm{v}^{2}_{k}:G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{2})\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}-\nabla\bm{v}^{2}:\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}\right)\varphi.\end{split}

(2.125)

The sequence
\{|\nabla\bm{v}^{1}_{k}:G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{2})\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}|\}_{k,m\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{1}(Q_{T})
thanks to (
2.105
) and (
2.17
). Moreover, due to the properties of
G
the sequence is bounded uniformly with respect to
k\in\mathbb{N}
in
L^{2}(Q_{T})
and it is nondecreasing in
m
almost everywhere in
Q_{T}
. Hence, for any fixed
m\in\mathbb{N}
and a suitable not relabeled subsequence, there exists

\overline{|\nabla\bm{v}^{1}:G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|}
satisfying as
k\to\infty

|\nabla\bm{v}_{k}^{1}:G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{2})\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}|\rightharpoonup\overline{|\nabla\bm{v}^{1}:G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|}\quad\mbox{ weakly in }L^{2}(Q_{T})

(2.126)

and (
l,n\in\mathbb{N}
)

\overline{|\nabla\bm{v}^{1}:G_{l}(|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|}\leq\overline{|\nabla\bm{v}^{1}:G_{n}(|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|}\quad\mbox{ a.e. in }Q_{T}\quad\mbox{ whenever }l\leq n.

By the monotone convergence theorem we then obtain the existence of the element
\overline{|\nabla\bm{v}^{1}:G_{\infty}(|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|}
satisfying as
m\to\infty

\overline{|\nabla\bm{v}^{1}:G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|}\to\overline{|\nabla\bm{v}^{1}:G_{\infty}(|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|}\quad\mbox{ strongly in }L^{1}(Q_{T}).

The modulus of the limit as
k\to\infty
and
m\to\infty
of the first integral in (
2.125
) is thus less than or equal to

\int_{Q_{T}\setminus E_{j}}\left(\overline{|\nabla\bm{v}^{1}:G_{\infty}(|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|}+|\nabla\bm{v}^{1}:\overline{\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}}|\right)\varphi.

(2.127)

Since the integrand in (
2.127
) belongs to
L^{1}(Q_{T})
and
|Q_{T}\setminus E_{j}|\to 0
as
j\to\infty
, we observe that the limit as
k\to\infty
,
m\to\infty
and
j\to\infty
of the first integral in (
2.125
) is equal to zero. Moreover, from (
2.59
), (
2.108
), (
2.109
), (
2.113
) and the definition of
G_{m}
, one can deduce that

\begin{split}\lim_{m\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{Q_{T}}\left(\nabla\bm{v}^{2}_{k}:G_{m}(|\mathbb{F}_{k}|^{2})\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}-\nabla\bm{v}^{2}:\mathbb{F}_{k}\mathbb{F}_{k}^{T}\right)\varphi\\
\leq\lim_{m\to\infty}\left(2m\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla\bm{v}^{2}_{k}-\nabla\bm{v}^{2}|\varphi\right)=0,\end{split}

(2.128)

i.e. the limit as
k\to\infty
and
m\to\infty
of the second integral in (
2.125
) is also equal to zero. To conclude (
2.124
) it remains to prove that

\lim_{j\to\infty}L_{3}^{2}=0.

However, since
L_{3}^{2}
is determined by (
2.101
) and (
2.112
), it follows straightforwardly from (
2.59
), (
2.105
), (
2.108
), (
2.109
) and the property
|Q_{T}\setminus E_{j}|\to 0
as
j\to\infty
. Hence (
2.124
) holds true. Note that the procedure how to prove (
2.124
) is in detail described in
[
14
]
.

To conclude, since
l_{2}=L_{1}+L_{2}+L_{3}
, we see, using (
2.102
), (
2.112
), (
2.123
) and (
2.124
), that (
2.98
) holds with

\tilde{L}:=1+2|\nabla\bm{v}|+R|\mathbb{F}|^{2}\in L^{2}(Q_{T}),

where
R:=\sup_{\theta>0}g(\theta)
. This completes the proof of the compactness of the sequence
\{\mathbb{F}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
in
(L^{2}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
.
∎

3
Existence of weak solutions

This section is devoted to the proof of the main result of the paper, namely the global-in-time existence result for large data. Before presenting the proof, we first state the result precisely. We consider here only the three-dimensional case; however, the argument extends to other dimensions with only minor modifications.

Theorem 3.9
.

Let the material parameters satisfy (
1.13
)–(
1.19
). Assume that

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{0}\in L^{2}_{\bm{n},\diver},\quad\mathbb{F}_{0}\in L^{2}(\Omega)^{3\times 3},\quad\theta_{0}\in L^{1}(\Omega),\quad\det\mathbb{F}_{0}\in L^{1}(\Omega)

(3.1)

and that
\det\mathbb{F}_{0}>0
almost everywhere in
\Omega
. Then there exists
(\bm{v},\theta,\mathbb{F})
satisfying (
2.7
)–(
2.11
).

In the present section, we shall make use of the already proven weak stability result and show the approximation procedure, which gives the existence of weak solutions. We want to remark, that the proof is already quite extensive with many approximating steps, therefore as to not make it even longer we shall assume some preliminary knowledge of the reader in the construction of approximating schemes and obtaining the energy estimates. Without going into much details we will often use the renormalization theory introduced by DiPerna and Lions
[
19
]
, to be able to properly test equations even without the proper regularity of the test function. We will also often assume that the reader understands how to apply a mix of weak and strong convergence results to converge in the weak formulations of equations in linear and non-linear terms. Let us begin our discussion with the introduction of an approximation procedure. Denote by
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{4},\varepsilon_{5},\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{7}\in(0,1)
some small constants, that will converge to
0^{+}
throughout the approximation. Fix
\{\omega_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\{\Gamma_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\beta_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
as the standard Galerkin basis’, which are orthonormal in
L^{2}
and orthogonal in
W^{s,2}_{\bm{n},\diver}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
W^{s,2}(\Omega)
,
W^{s,2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3})
respectively, for
s
large enough so that

\displaystyle W^{s-1,2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}.

Note that we also fix the appropriate boundary conditions for them which are related to the variables
\bm{v},e,\mathbb{F}
, that is

\bm{v}\cdot\bm{n}=0,\quad\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}\cdot\bm{n}=0,\quad\text{ and }\nabla_{x}e\cdot\bm{n}=0.

Then, define

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{l}:=\sum_{i=1}^{l}a_{i}(t)\omega_{i}(x),\quad e^{m}:=\sum_{i=1}^{m}b_{i}(t)\Gamma_{i}(x),\quad\mathbb{F}^{n}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}c_{i}(t)\beta_{i}(x),

with initial conditions
\bm{v}^{l}_{0}:=P^{l}\bm{v}_{0}
,
e^{m}_{0}=P^{m}(e^{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{6}}_{0})
,
\mathbb{F}^{n}_{0}=P^{n}(T^{\det}_{\varepsilon_{5}}(T_{\varepsilon_{3}}(\mathbb{F}_{0}))\star\varphi_{\varepsilon_{7}})
, where
P^{i}
is a projection onto first
i
vectors of a respective Galerkin basis,
e^{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{6}}_{0}
is a cut-off from below for
e_{0}
, that is

\displaystyle e_{0}^{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{6}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}e_{0},\text{ whenever }e_{0}\geq\min\{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{6}\}\\
1,\text{ otherwise},\end{array}\right.

T^{\det}_{\varepsilon_{5}}
is a cut-off from below over the determinant, that is

\displaystyle T^{\det}_{\varepsilon_{5}}(F):=F\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\det F\geq\varepsilon_{5}\}}+\mathbb{I}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\det F<\varepsilon_{5}\}},

T_{\varepsilon_{3}}
is the typical truncation at the level
2/\varepsilon_{3}
adapted to matrices, that is

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon_{3}}(F):=F\,\mathbf{1}_{\{|F|\leq 2/\varepsilon_{3}\}}+\mathbb{I}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{|F|>2/\varepsilon_{3}\}},

\varphi_{\varepsilon_{7}}
is the standard mollification kernel, i.e.
\varphi
is a smooth, non-negative function, compactly supported in a ball of radius one and fulfills
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=1
. Then, we set
\varphi_{\varepsilon_{7}}(x)=\frac{1}{{\varepsilon_{7}}^{d}}\varphi\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon_{7}}\right)
. Moreover, to define an appropriate approximating system let

\displaystyle g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(z):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{linear}&\text{on }(0,\varepsilon_{1}),\\
\text{smooth}&\text{on }[\varepsilon_{1},2\varepsilon_{1}],\\
g(z)&\text{on }(2\varepsilon_{1},+\infty),\end{array}\right.

satisfying the condition
g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(0)=0
. With this, define

\displaystyle e^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\theta,F)=\theta+\underbrace{(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)-\theta g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta))}_{\text{extended by }0\text{ for }\theta<0}(\mathrm{tr}(F\,F^{T})-3-\ln((\det(F\,F^{T})-\varepsilon_{2})_{+}+\varepsilon_{2})),

(3.2)

and by
\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e,F)
denote the inverse of the above function with respect to
\theta
, i. e.:

\displaystyle\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\theta,F),F)\equiv\theta.

Lastly, we define as well

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(s):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&|s|\leq 1/\varepsilon_{3},\\
0,&|s|\geq 2/\varepsilon_{3},\\
\text{smooth, }&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}:=2\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|F|)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)F\,F^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}+\nu(\theta)\mathbb{D}\bm{v}.

Having all the definitions we are ready to state our approximating system

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l}+\diver_{x}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\bm{v}^{l}|^{2})\bm{v}^{l}\otimes\bm{v}^{l})-\diver_{x}\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}),\mathbb{F}^{n},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l})=0,

(3.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}^{n}+\Diver_{x}(\mathbb{F}^{n}\otimes\bm{v}^{l})-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{n}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{n}\frac{(\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n})-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n})}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,\,-\varepsilon_{4}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{n}+\frac{\tau(\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}))}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{n}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{n}}(\mathbb{F}^{n}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}\,\mathbb{F}^{n}-\mathbb{F}^{n})=0,

(3.4)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e^{m}+\diver_{x}(e^{m}\bm{v}^{l})-\varepsilon_{7}\Delta_{x}e^{m}-\diver_{x}(\kappa(\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}))\nabla_{x}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}),\mathbb{F}^{n},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}=0.

(3.5)

Note that the equations above should be understood as the projection onto the first, respectively
l,n,m
vectors of the Galerkin basis, which is a standard Galerkin formulation. For the sake of simplicity we have written them in the differential form. For brevity we note that the existence of the solutions is a standard procedure involving the Carathéodory theorem of existence. Moreover, every function actually depends on all of the parameters
l,n,m,\varepsilon_{1},...,\varepsilon_{7}
, but to avoid the overload of indices we shall only keep the index related to the Galerkin approximation (when applicable), and the one with respect to which, we are currently considering the convergence.

Step 1: convergence with
m\to+\infty
.
It is easy to see that since we keep the Galerkin index for
\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}
and
\bm{v}^{l}_{m}
fixed, both of those sequences are bounded in
W^{1,\infty}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}(\Omega))
, for interested readers we also refer to
[
4
, Section 4, under (4.16)]
. Hence, by the Banach–Alaoglu theorem and the Aubin–Lions lemma (up to the subsequence which we do not relabel),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l}_{m}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}_{\mathbf{n},\diver}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}^{n},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;(W^{1,\infty}(\Omega))^{3\times 3}),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{l}_{m}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v}^{l},

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }C([0,T];W^{1,\infty}_{\mathbf{n},\diver}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{F}^{n},

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }C([0,T];(W^{1,\infty}(\Omega))^{3\times 3}),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{l}_{m}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v}^{l},

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T},

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{F}^{n},

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

Thus, we move forward to consider the convergence of
e^{m}
. Since we work with Galerkin approximation we can test (
3.5
) by
e^{m}
, and obtain the following energy equality

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|e^{m}(t)|^{2}&\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon_{7}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}e^{m}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&+\int_{\Omega}\kappa\nabla_{x}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}\cdot\nabla_{x}e^{m}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}(\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},...,\varepsilon_{6}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{m})e^{m}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

(3.6)

Due to the uniform bounds on
\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m},\bm{v}^{l}_{m}
, we deduce a straightforward bound using Hölder’s inequality

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}(\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},...,\varepsilon_{6}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{m})e^{m}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq\|\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},...,\varepsilon_{6}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|e^{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.7)

Let us move forward to a more problematic term involving the gradient of the function
\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}
. First, note that

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})=\nabla_{e}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})\nabla_{x}e^{m}+\nabla_{F}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}.

(3.8)

We will focus on both the terms separately. By the Inverse Function theorem

\displaystyle\nabla_{e}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})=(\nabla_{\theta}e^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}),\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}))^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})g^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}^{*}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}))\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})=\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})-3-\ln((\det(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})-\varepsilon_{2})_{+}+\varepsilon_{2}).

(3.9)

Note that whenever
\det(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})\leq\varepsilon_{2}
, then (fixing
\varepsilon_{2}>0
small enough)

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})-3-\ln((\det(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})-\varepsilon_{2})_{+}+\varepsilon_{2}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})-3-\ln\varepsilon_{2}\geq 0,

and for
\det(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})>\varepsilon_{2}
we can use a classical inequality true for any symmetric matrix (cf.
[
4
, Lemma A.3]
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})-3-\ln((\det(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})-\varepsilon_{2})_{+}+\varepsilon_{2}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})-3-\ln\det(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})\geq 0.

Moreover by the definition of
g_{\varepsilon_{1}}
and the concavity of
g
we have

g^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)\leq 0,\text{ and }g^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)=0,\text{ whenever }\theta<\varepsilon_{1}.

Hence,

\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})g^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}^{*}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}))\leq 0.

Thus,

\displaystyle 0\leq\nabla_{e}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})\leq 1.

(3.10)

For the second term we obtain the equality

\begin{split}&\left|\nabla_{F}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})\right|=\left|\frac{\nabla_{F}e^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}),\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})}{\nabla_{\theta}e^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}),\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})}\right|\\
&\leq\left|\mathbb{I}-(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\det(\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}_{m})>\varepsilon_{2}}\right|\leq 3+\frac{C}{\varepsilon_{2}}\|\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\|^{4}_{\infty}.\end{split}

(3.11)

Combining (
3.7
), (
3.10
), (
3.11
), as well as the bounds on
\kappa
and the known bounds on
\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m},\bm{v}^{l}_{m}
, together with (
3.6
) and the Young inequality we obtain

\begin{split}&\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|e^{m}(t)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon_{7}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}e^{m}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad=-\int_{\Omega}\kappa\nabla_{x}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}\cdot\nabla_{x}e^{m}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}(\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},...,\varepsilon_{6}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{m})e^{m}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq\int_{\Omega}\kappa\left|\nabla_{F}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\cdot\nabla_{x}e^{m}\right|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\|\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},...,\varepsilon_{6}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|e^{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\leq C(K,\varepsilon_{2},\|\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\|_{\infty})\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\cdot\nabla_{x}e^{m}\right|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+C(|\Omega|,\|\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\|_{L^{4}},\|\bm{v}^{l}_{m}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})\|e^{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\leq C(K,\varepsilon_{2},\|\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\|_{\infty},\varepsilon_{7})\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\frac{\varepsilon_{7}}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}e^{m}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+C(|\Omega|,\|\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\|_{L^{4}},\|\bm{v}^{l}_{m}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})+\|e^{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\\
&\leq C(K,|\Omega|,\varepsilon_{2},\|\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\|_{\infty},\varepsilon_{7},\|\bm{v}^{l}_{m}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})+\frac{\varepsilon_{7}}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}e^{m}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\|e^{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\end{split}

The inequality above together with Grönwall’s inequality imply readily that
\{e^{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
, and
L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega))
. Moreover, one can test (
3.5
) by
\partial_{t}e^{m}
to obtain
\{\partial_{t}e^{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
. Since the calculation are analogous to the made above, we skip them. Thus, by the Banach–Alaoglu theorem as well as the Aubin–Lions lemma we may deduce the existence of
e\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega))
with
\partial_{t}e\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
such that (up to the subsequence, which we do not relabel)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e^{m}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle e^{m}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle e^{m}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle e^{m}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{2}(Q_{T}),

\displaystyle e^{m}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

All that is left to do is to identify the limit of
\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})
. Note that by (
3.8
), (
3.10
), (
3.11
), and the already established bounds on
\{\nabla_{x}e^{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
we know that
\{\nabla_{x}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(Q_{T})
. Moreover by a similar argument we can see that
\{\partial_{t}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(Q_{T})
as well. Using the equation

\displaystyle\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})=\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m}_{0},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m,0})+\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m}(s,x),\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}(s,x))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s,

we can deduce that
\{\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(Q_{T})
as long as
\{\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m}_{0},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m,0})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is. By (
3.10
), (
3.11
) we know that
\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})
is Lipschitz with respect to its variables. Hence,

\displaystyle|\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m}_{0},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m,0})|\leq C(1+|e^{m}_{0}|+|\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m,0}|)

and by the boundedness of
\{e^{m}_{0}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m,0}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
we get what is needed. Thus, by the Banach–Alaoglu theorem and the Aubin–Lions lemma we can imply that there exists
\theta\in L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega))
with
\partial_{t}\theta\in L^{2}(Q_{T})
such that (up to the subsequence which we do not relabel)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(Q_{T}),

\displaystyle\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{2}(Q_{T}),

\displaystyle\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e^{m},\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

At the same time, we can identify
\theta
in another way. Since
\{e^{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\mathbb{F}^{n}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
converge a.e. to their limits and
\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}
is continuous with respect to the variables we can see that

\displaystyle\theta(t,x)=\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e,\mathbb{F}^{n}).

Moreover, converging in (
3.2
) grants us

\begin{split}e(t,x)&=\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e,\mathbb{F}^{n})+\underbrace{(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e,\mathbb{F}^{n}))-\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e,\mathbb{F}^{n})g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(e,\mathbb{F}^{n})))}_{\text{extended by }0\text{ for }\theta<0}\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{F}^{n}\mathbb{F}^{n,T})\\
&=\theta(t,x)+\underbrace{(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x))-\theta(t,x)g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x)))}_{\text{extended by }0\text{ for }\theta<0}\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{F}^{n}\mathbb{F}^{n,T}),\end{split}

(3.12)

where
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
is defined in (
3.9
). Having (
3.12
) we will drop
e^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}
and
\theta^{*}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}
notation, as we introduced it only to obtain this equation in the limit to connect the internal energy and the temperature. Having all of the proven convergences it is easy to see that we may converge in (
3.3
)-(
3.5
) to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l}+\diver_{x}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\bm{v}^{l}|^{2})\bm{v}^{l}\otimes\bm{v}^{l})-\diver_{x}\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F}^{n},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l})=0,

(3.13)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}^{n}+\Diver_{x}(\mathbb{F}^{n}\otimes\bm{v}^{l})-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{n}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{n}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,\,-\varepsilon_{4}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{n}+\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{n}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{n}}(\mathbb{F}^{n}\,\mathbb{F}^{n,T}\,\mathbb{F}^{n}-\mathbb{F}^{n})=0,

(3.14)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e+\diver_{x}(e\bm{v}^{l})-\varepsilon_{7}\Delta_{x}e-\diver_{x}(\kappa(\theta)\nabla_{x}\theta)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F}^{n},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}=0,

(3.15)

where (
3.13
) and (
3.14
) are to be understood as projections onto the Galerkin basis, and (
3.15
) is to be understood in the weak sense in the sense of an operator on a predual space to the time derivative (in this case
\partial_{t}e\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
, but we shall refer the reader to the particular convergences in the later steps to verify the predual space, as it changes multiple times).

Step 2: convergence with
n\to+\infty
.
As in the previous step, it is easy to obtain (sine
l
is fixed) a subsequence (which we do not relabel) that satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l}_{n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}_{\mathbf{n},\diver}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{l}_{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v}^{l},

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }C([0,T];W^{1,\infty}_{\mathbf{n},\diver}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{l}_{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v}^{l},

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

Moreover, as we work with Galerkin projections, we can test (
3.14
) by
\mathbb{F}^{n}
to get
\{\mathbb{F}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;(L^{2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})\cap L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})
. As the argument here is fairly simple as long as
\{\bm{v}^{l}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}(\Omega))
, we skip it for the sake of brevity. We will instead focus on showing the bound
\{\mathbb{F}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
. To do so let

\displaystyle C(t):=M\,e^{Kt},

where
K
comes from (
2.7
), and
M>\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{3}}
is to be fixed later, and define
\mathbb{H}^{n}(t,x):=(|\mathbb{F}^{n}(t,x)|-C(t))_{+}
,
\mathbb{G}^{n}(t,x):=\frac{\mathbb{F}^{n}}{|\mathbb{F}^{n}|}\mathbb{H}^{n}
. Note that
\mathbb{G}^{n}
belongs to
L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})
, which means we may test (
3.14
) by
P^{n}\mathbb{G}^{n}
. Let us treat all the terms appearing there separately. For the time derivative we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{F}^{n}:P^{n}\mathbb{G}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{F}^{n}:\mathbb{G}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{H}^{n}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+C^{\prime}(t)\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{H}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.

(3.16)

Moving forward, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{F}^{n}\otimes\bm{v}^{l}_{n}:\nabla_{x}P^{n}\mathbb{G}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{F}^{n}\otimes\bm{v}^{l}:\nabla_{x}\mathbb{G}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\sum_{i,j,k}\int_{\Omega}\bm{v}^{l}_{n,i}\partial_{x_{i}}\mathbb{F}^{n}_{jk}\mathbb{G}^{n}_{jk}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j,k}\int_{\Omega}\bm{v}^{l}_{n,i}\partial_{x_{i}}|\mathbb{F}^{n}_{jk}|\mathbb{H}^{n}_{jk}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i}\int_{\Omega}\bm{v}^{l}_{n,i}\partial_{x_{i}}|\mathbb{H}^{n}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=0,

and as
\Lambda_{3}(|\mathbb{F}^{n}|)=0
, whenever
|\mathbb{F}^{n}|>2/\varepsilon_{3}

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{n}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{l}_{n}\mathbb{F}^{n}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}:P^{n}\mathbb{G}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right|\\
&\quad=\left|\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{n}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{l}_{n}\mathbb{F}^{n}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}:\mathbb{G}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right|=0,\end{split}

as well as

\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{n}:\nabla_{x}P^{n}\mathbb{G}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{n}:\nabla_{x}\mathbb{G}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\int_{\Omega}\left(1-\frac{C(t)}{|\mathbb{F}^{n}|}\right)_{+}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{n}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathbb{F}^{n}|\geq C(t)\}}\frac{C(t)}{|\mathbb{F}^{n}|^{3}}\sum_{i}|\mathbb{F}^{n}:\partial_{x_{i}}\mathbb{F}^{n}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\geq 0\end{split}

Furthermore

\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega}\frac{\tau(\theta^{n})}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{n}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{n}}\mathbb{F}^{n}\mathbb{F}^{n,T}\mathbb{F}^{n}:P^{n}\mathbb{G}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\tau(\theta^{n})}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{n}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{n}}\frac{|\mathbb{F}^{n}\mathbb{F}^{n,T}|^{2}}{|\mathbb{F}^{n}|}\mathbb{H}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\geq 0,\end{split}

and using (
1.14
)

\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega}\frac{\tau(\theta^{n})}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{n}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{n}}\mathbb{F}^{n}:P^{n}\mathbb{G}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\tau(\theta^{n})}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{n}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{n}}|\mathbb{F}^{n}|\mathbb{H}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq K\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{H}^{n}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+KC(t)\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{H}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

Combining all of the above, we obtain an inequality

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{H}^{n}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+C^{\prime}(t)\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{H}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq K\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{H}^{n}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+KC(t)\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{H}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq.K\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{H}^{n}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+MKC(t)\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{H}^{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\end{split}

Since
C^{\prime}(t)=MKC(t)
the linear terms cancel and we are left with

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{H}^{n}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\leq K\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{H}^{n}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,

which by Grönwall’s inequality implies

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{H}^{n}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\leq e^{2TK}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{H}^{n}_{0}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.

Note that since

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{F}^{n}_{0}\|_{\infty}=\|P^{n}(T^{\det}_{\varepsilon_{5}}(T_{\varepsilon_{3}}(\mathbb{F}_{0}))\star\varphi_{\varepsilon_{7}})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|P^{n}(T^{\det}_{\varepsilon_{5}}(T_{\varepsilon_{3}}(\mathbb{F}_{0}))\star\varphi_{\varepsilon_{7}})\|_{W^{s,2}}\leq\|T^{\det}_{\varepsilon_{5}}(T_{\varepsilon_{3}}(\mathbb{F}_{0}))\star\varphi_{\varepsilon_{7}}\|_{W^{s,2}},

by defining
M:=\max\{2/\varepsilon_{3},\|T^{\det}_{\varepsilon_{5}}(T_{\varepsilon_{3}}(\mathbb{F}_{0}))\star\varphi_{\varepsilon_{7}}\|_{W^{s,2}}\}
, we know that
\mathbb{H}^{n}_{0}=0
. Thus, we deduce

\mathbb{H}^{n}\equiv 0,\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

Hence,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{F}^{n}(t,x)|\leq C(t)=M\,e^{Kt},

(3.17)

and we have what we postulated. As a comment we want to mention that a similar bound will hold for a weak formulation of
\mathbb{F}
in further arguments, as without the projection
P^{n}
one can simply utilize the Young convolution inequality to deduce

\|\mathbb{F}_{0}\|_{\infty}=\|T^{\det}_{\varepsilon_{5}}(T_{\varepsilon_{3}}(\mathbb{F}_{0}))\star\varphi_{\varepsilon_{7}}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\varphi_{\varepsilon_{7}}\|_{1}\|T^{\det}_{\varepsilon_{5}}(T_{\varepsilon_{3}}(\mathbb{F}_{0}))\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{3}}.

In consequence, one will obtain a similar bound but with a constant

\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{3}}\,e^{Kt}.

Thus, whenever
\varepsilon_{3}
is fixed, we can utilize the bound in
L^{\infty}
on any sequence
\mathbb{F}
. Going back to the main argument, we can obtain in a similar manner as in the proof of Corollary
2.8
that
\{\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;((W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})^{*})
. Thus, by the Banach–Alaoglu theorem and the Aubin–Lions lemma we can find a subsequence (which we do not relabel) for which

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}^{n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;((W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})^{*}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3},

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }(L^{q}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3},\ q<+\infty,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

To move forward, note that the bounds established for
e^{n}
in Step 1 are independent of
n
. The only one that does not hold is
\{\partial_{t}e^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
. Here, using the equation (
3.15
) one deduces that
\{\partial_{t}e^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{*})
. Thus, similarly as before we imply the convergences

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e^{n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{*}),

\displaystyle e^{n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle e^{n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle e^{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{2}(Q_{T}),

\displaystyle e^{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

Analogously

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta^{n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{*}),

\displaystyle\theta^{n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\theta^{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{2}(Q_{T}),

\displaystyle\theta^{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

All of the convergences allow us to converge in (
3.13
)–(
3.15
) and obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l}+\diver_{x}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\bm{v}^{l}|^{2})\bm{v}^{l}\otimes\bm{v}^{l})-\diver_{x}\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l})=0,

(3.18)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}+\Diver_{x}(\mathbb{F}\otimes\bm{v}^{l})-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{l}\mathbb{F}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,\,-\varepsilon_{4}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}+\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}-\mathbb{F})=0,

(3.19)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e+\diver_{x}(e\bm{v}^{l})-\varepsilon_{7}\Delta_{x}e-\diver_{x}(\kappa(\theta)\nabla_{x}\theta)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}=0,

(3.20)

where (
3.18
) is to be understood as projections onto the Galerkin basis, and (
3.19
), (
3.20
) are to be understood in the weak sense in the sense of an operator on a predual space to the time derivative. Note that from (
3.12
) we keep

\begin{split}e(t,x)=\theta(t,x)+\underbrace{(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x))-\theta(t,x)g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x)))}_{\text{extended by }0\text{ for }\theta<0}\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}),\end{split}

(3.21)

for
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
defined as in (
3.9
).

Step 3:
\theta\geq\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\}
.
Here, note that whenever
\theta<\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\}
by (
3.21
) we know that
e=\theta
. Now test (
3.20
) by
-\mathbf{1}_{(0,\tau)}(\theta-\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\})_{-}
. We can do so only formally but we skip the approximation procedure for the sake of brevity. We get

\begin{split}&\|(\theta-\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\})_{-}(\tau)\|_{2}^{2}-\|(\theta-\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\})_{-}(0)\|_{2}^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{7}+\kappa(\theta))|\nabla_{x}(\theta-\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\})_{-}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&=-\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}(\theta-\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\})_{-}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\leq 0.\end{split}

As by definition
e_{0}^{n}\geq\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\}
(again by (
3.21
) if there would be points at which
\theta_{0}^{n}<\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\}
, then it would follow that
e_{0}^{n}<\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\}
as well), we deduce

\|(\theta-\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\})_{-}(\tau)\|_{2}=0,

and in consequence
\theta\geq\min\{\varepsilon_{6},\varepsilon_{1}\}
.

Step 4: convergence with
\varepsilon_{7}\to 0^{+}
.
As in the previous step, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l}_{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}_{\mathbf{n},\diver}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{l}_{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v}^{l},

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }C([0,T];W^{1,\infty}_{\mathbf{n},\diver}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{l}_{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v}^{l},

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

Moreover, it is easy to see that (
3.19
) can be tested by
\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}
(the only slight issue is the regularity in time, but one can mollify in the time variable, we skip the classical argument for the sake of brevity), which means that we again obtain the bounds of
\{\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\}_{\varepsilon_{7}>0}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;(L^{2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})\cap L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})
. To see that this sequence is bounded in
(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
one can repeat the argument given from (
3.16
) to (
3.17
), see also comments under (
3.17
). Hence,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;((W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})^{*}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3},

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }(L^{q}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3},\ q<+\infty,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

To move forward, we will obtain proper bounds on
\{\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}\}_{\varepsilon_{7}>0}
. First, test (
3.20
) by
1
, which gives us
\{e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\}_{\varepsilon_{7}>0}
bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega))
, thus from (
3.21
), the non-negativity of the function
\theta\mapsto g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)-\theta g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)
, and non-negativity of
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
, we obtain that the same bound is true for
\{\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}\}_{\varepsilon_{7}>0}
. Furthermore, by Step 3
\frac{1}{\theta}
is a proper test function, hence we can follow Proposition
1.1
to obtain the analogue of (
1.32
), i.e.

\begin{split}&\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\eta^{\varepsilon_{7}}_{\lambda}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon_{7}\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad-\varepsilon_{4}\int_{\Omega}(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda}+g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1})\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}):(\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7},T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad-\varepsilon_{4}\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{x}(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda}+g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1})):(\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7},T}+\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7},T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad-\int_{\Omega}\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}}((\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}):\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad+\int_{\Omega}2(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\mathbb{I}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{\varepsilon_{7}}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|)\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}}(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&=(1-\lambda)\int_{\Omega}\kappa\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{2-\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}2\nu\frac{|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{2-\lambda}}+\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})((\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}):\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{1-\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

(3.22)

where
\lambda\in(0,1)
, and
h_{\lambda}
is defined in Lemma
2.6
. Note that by boundedness of
\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}
and
\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{\varepsilon_{7}}
in
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
we have (
2.23
) for free (in fact,
L^{2}(Q_{T})
bounds for both of them would be enough), and one can operate the same way as in the proof of (
2.36
) to bound any terms without
\varepsilon_{7},\varepsilon_{4}
in front of them. Indeed, without loss of generality we shall assume
\varepsilon_{2}<\varepsilon_{5}^{2}
(as we will converge with
\varepsilon_{2}\to 0^{+}
before doing so with
\varepsilon_{5}
), which implies

\displaystyle\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}}((\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}):\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})=\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}}|\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\mathbb{I}|^{2}.

(3.23)

Thus, we may bound

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{\Omega}\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}}((\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}):\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right|\\
&\leq K\|(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda})\|_{\infty}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\mathbb{I}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

(3.24)

and

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{\Omega}2(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\mathbb{I}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}}(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right|\\
&\leq\|(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda})\|_{\infty}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}-\mathbb{I}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right).\end{split}

(3.25)

Now, let us focus on those additional terms. For the first one, using (
3.21
) we get

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{7}\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=-\varepsilon_{7}(1-\lambda)\int_{\Omega}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-2}|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+\varepsilon_{7}(1-\lambda)\int_{\Omega}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1}g^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad-\varepsilon_{7}(1-\lambda)\int_{\Omega}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-2}(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}))\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})\nabla_{x}\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

The first term can be put on the right-hand side of (
3.22
) as it is negative, the same can be said for the second term as
g_{\varepsilon_{1}}
is concave. For the third one we can estimate using Young’s inequality

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{7}(1-\lambda)\left|\int_{\Omega}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-2}(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}))\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})\nabla_{x}\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right|\\
&\leq(1-\lambda)\frac{\varepsilon_{7}}{2}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{2-\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+2\varepsilon_{7}(1-\lambda)\|(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda/2-1}(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}))\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})\|^{2}_{\infty}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&=:I_{1}+I_{2}.\end{split}

(3.26)

The term
I_{1}
can be absorbed into the right-hand side of (
3.22
). For
I_{2}
we observe, that from Step 3 the sequence
(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda/2-1}
is bounded, by definition
g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})
is bounded, and from (
1.19
)
\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
as well. Moreover, by the boundedness of
\{\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\}_{\varepsilon_{7}>0}
in
(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
the same is true for
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})
, and from boundedness of
\{\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\}_{\varepsilon_{7}>0}
in
(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}\cap L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})
, the integral
\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x
is bounded as well. Hence,
I_{2}
is uniformly bounded in
\varepsilon_{7}
. Now we can move to bounding the terms with
\varepsilon_{4}
in front of them in (
3.22
). Again, we note that by analogous arguments as above the functions
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})
and
h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda}+g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1}
are bounded in
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
, hence

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{4}\left|\int_{\Omega}(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda}+g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1})\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}):(\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7},T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right|\\
&\leq\varepsilon_{4}\|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda}+g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1})\|_{\infty}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq C(\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3})\varepsilon_{4}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

(3.27)

which is bounded uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon_{7}
. Moving further, we bound using Young’s inequality and (
1.15
)

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{4}\left|\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{x}(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda}+g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1})):(\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7},T}+\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7},T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right|\\
&\leq\varepsilon_{4}\int_{\Omega}|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda}+g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1}))||\nabla_{x}\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+2\varepsilon_{4}\int_{\Omega}|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})||\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}||(1-\lambda)g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1}+(\lambda-1)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-2}||\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}||\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq\varepsilon_{4}\|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda}+g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1}))\|_{\infty}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+\frac{4\varepsilon_{4}}{K^{-1}}(1-\lambda)\|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}(g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda/2}-g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda/2-1})\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+(1-\lambda)\frac{K^{-1}}{2}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{2-\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq\varepsilon_{4}\|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda}+g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda-1}))\|_{\infty}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+\frac{4\varepsilon_{4}}{K^{-1}}(1-\lambda)\|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{7}})\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}(g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda/2}-g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda/2-1})\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+(1-\lambda)\int_{\Omega}\frac{\kappa}{2}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{2-\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq C(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6},K,\lambda)\varepsilon_{4}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+(1-\lambda)\int_{\Omega}\frac{\kappa}{2}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{2-\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\end{split}

(3.28)

Here, we have bounded the first two terms with similar arguments as below (
3.26
), and the third one can be absorbed into the right-hand side of (
3.22
). Combining all of our considerations from (
3.26
) to (
3.28
) together with (
3.22
) we obtain an inequality similar to (
2.37
), that is

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\leq C,\qquad\lambda\in(1,2),

(3.29)

and
C>0
is independent of
\varepsilon_{7}
. With this, we may finally obtain proper bounds on
\{e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\}_{\varepsilon_{7}>0}
. We test (
3.20
) by
e^{\varepsilon_{7}}
to obtain

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|e^{\varepsilon_{7}}(t)|^{2}&\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon_{7}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&+\int_{\Omega}\kappa\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}(\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{\varepsilon_{7}})e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

(3.30)

The term on the right-hand side follows the same inequality as in (
3.7
). Moreover, using (
3.21
) we may write

\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega}\kappa\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\kappa|\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\kappa\,\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}g^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad-\int_{\Omega}\kappa\,(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}))\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\,\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7},T})\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

(3.31)

Then, using (
1.15
), (
3.29
), the properties of the function
g_{\varepsilon_{1}}
, as well as the
L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega))
bounds on
\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}
and the Young inequality, we may deduce

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{\Omega}\kappa\,\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}g^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}\kappa\,(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}))\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\,\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7},T})\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}\cdot\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right|\\
&\leq 16K\|(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda/2+1}g^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})\|^{2}_{\infty}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})^{\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\frac{\kappa}{4}|\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+16K\|(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}})-\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}))\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}})\|^{2}_{\infty}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\frac{\kappa}{4}|\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq C+\int_{\Omega}\frac{\kappa}{2}|\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

(3.32)

where
C>0
is independent of
\varepsilon_{7}
, and
\lambda
in the considerations was taken from the interval
(1,2)
. Hence, combining (
3.31
) and (
3.32
) together with (
3.30
) we get

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|e^{\varepsilon_{7}}(t)|^{2}&\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon_{7}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\frac{\kappa}{2}|\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\leq C+\|e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2},\end{split}

which by Grönwall’s inequality provides the bounds on
\{e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\}_{\varepsilon_{7}}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega))
. Note also that the bound on
\varepsilon_{7}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t
will allow us to converge
\varepsilon_{7}\Delta_{x}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\rightarrow 0
, in the sense of distributions in (
3.20
). Similarly as in Step 2, we may deduce from the equation boundedness of
\{\partial_{t}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}\}_{\varepsilon_{7}>0}
in
L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{*})
. Having this, we can see that from the equation (
3.21
), we can deduce the bounds on
\{\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}\}_{\varepsilon_{7}>0}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega))
and
\{\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}\}_{\varepsilon_{7}>0}
in
L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{*})
. At last, using the Banach–Alaoglu theorem and the Aubin–Lions lemma we obtain the following convergences

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{*}),

\displaystyle e^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle e^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle e^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{2}(Q_{T}),

\displaystyle e^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T},

and

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{*}),

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{2}(Q_{T}),

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon_{7}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

Similarly as before, the obtained convergences allow us to converge from (
3.18
)-(
3.20
) to the system

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l}+\diver_{x}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\bm{v}^{l}|^{2})\bm{v}^{l}\otimes\bm{v}^{l})-\diver_{x}\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l})=0,

(3.33)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}+\Diver_{x}(\mathbb{F}\otimes\bm{v}^{l})-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{l}\mathbb{F}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}-\varepsilon_{4}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}+\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}-\mathbb{F})=0,

(3.34)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e+\diver_{x}(e\bm{v}^{l})-\diver_{x}(\kappa(\theta)\nabla_{x}\theta)-\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}=0,

(3.35)

where (
3.33
) is to be understood as projections onto the Galerkin basis, and (
3.34
), (
3.35
) are to be understood in the weak sense in the sense of an operator on a predual space to the time derivative. Note that from (
3.21
) we keep

\begin{split}e(t,x)=\theta(t,x)+(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x))-\theta(t,x)g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x)))\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}),\end{split}

(3.36)

for
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
defined as in (
3.9
).

Step 5: convergence with
l\to+\infty
.
Let us start with the bounds on velocity. Since (
3.33
) is of Galerkin form, we may test it by
\bm{v}^{l}
to obtain the energy equality

\begin{split}\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}^{l}(t)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}^{l}(0)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}|\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}S(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s,\end{split}

(3.37)

as the boundary term is negative we can drop it, and using (
1.15
) together with Young’s inequality we may deduce

\begin{split}\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}^{l}(t)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+K^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{2K}{\varepsilon^{2}_{3}}|Q_{T}|+\frac{K^{-1}}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}^{l}(0)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

which readily implies that
\{\bm{v}^{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}_{\bm{n},\diver}(\Omega))
. Moreover, following the reasoning in Corollary
2.8
we can see that
\{\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}_{\bm{n},\diver}(\Omega))^{*})
. Thus, by the Banach–Alaoglu theorem, the Aubin–Lions lemma, and the interpolation, we get that (up to the subsequence)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{l}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}_{\bm{n},\diver}(\Omega))^{*}),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{l}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{l}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}_{\bm{n},\diver}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{l}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<10/3,

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{l}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

Furthermore, by the same argument as in the previous step, we obtain the convergences for the elastic tensor

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}^{l}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{2}(0,T;((W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})^{*}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{l}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{l}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3},

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{l}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }(L^{q}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3},\ q<+\infty,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{l}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

Note that we only lost here some regularity on the time derivative of
\mathbb{F}
, due to the loss of the
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
bounds on the gradient of the velocity. At this point we need to establish compactness of the temperature sequence
\{\theta^{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
. Due to the loss of the regularity of the symmetric gradient of the velocity, we cannot use the simple technique of testing (
3.35
) by
e^{l}
, since the term
\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}
is not bounded in
L^{2}(Q_{T})
. Thus, we come back to the technique presented in the Subsection
2.2.1
about almost everywhere convergence of the temperature. Let us begin with the observation, that one can still derive (
3.22
) but with
\varepsilon_{7}=0
, that is

\begin{split}&\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\eta^{l}_{\lambda}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad-\varepsilon_{4}\int_{\Omega}(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{l})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})(\theta^{l})^{\lambda}+g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})(\theta^{l})^{\lambda-1})\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l}):(\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l,T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad-\varepsilon_{4}\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{x}(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l})(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{l})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})(\theta^{l})^{\lambda}+g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})(\theta^{l})^{\lambda-1})):(\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{l,T}+\mathbb{F}^{l}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l,T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad-\int_{\Omega}\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{l}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{l}}((\mathbb{B}^{l})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{l}):\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l})(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{l})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})(\theta^{l})^{\lambda})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad+\int_{\Omega}2(\mathbb{B}^{l}-\mathbb{I}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{l}|)\frac{(\theta^{l}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{l}}(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{l})-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})(\theta^{l})^{\lambda})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
