&=(1-\lambda)\int_{\Omega}\kappa\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{l}|^{2}}{(\theta^{l})^{2-\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}2\nu\frac{|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}}{(\theta^{l})^{2-\lambda}}+\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{l}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{l}}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})((\mathbb{B}^{l})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{l}):\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l})}{(\theta^{l})^{1-\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

(3.38)

Furthermore, the established bounds (
3.24
), (
3.25
), (
3.27
), (
3.28
) are independent of
l
, which means that (
3.29
) holds as well, that is

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{l}|^{2}}{(\theta^{l})^{\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\leq C(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6},K,\lambda)\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+C(\lambda,K,\varepsilon_{3}),\qquad\lambda\in(1,2),

(3.39)

where
C
is independent of
l
, and following the argument under (
2.37
) we can deduce that
\{\theta^{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{q}(Q_{T})
for
q<5/3
and
\{\nabla_{x}\theta^{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{q}(Q_{T})
for
q<5/4
. With this, we wish to follow the argument in Subsection
2.2.1
, which means that we need the equations for the entropy
\eta^{l}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
. At this point of the approximation, the functions
1/\theta
and
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
are still proper test functions, hence following the argument in Proposition
1.1
one can verify that

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\eta^{l}+\diver_{x}(\eta^{l}\bm{v}^{l})-\diver_{x}\left(\kappa\frac{\nabla_{x}\theta^{l}}{\theta^{l}}\right)+\varepsilon_{4}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})}{\theta^{l}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l}):(\mathbb{F}^{l}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l,T}+\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{l,T})\\
&=\frac{\kappa|\nabla_{x}\theta^{l}|^{2}}{(\theta^{l})^{2}}+\frac{2\nu|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}}{\theta^{l}}+\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{l}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{l}}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}^{l}):((\mathbb{B}^{l})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{l})}{\theta^{l}},\end{split}

(3.40)

in the sense of operators on
C^{1}([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega})
, for
\eta^{l}=\ln\theta^{l}-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l})
, and following the procedure under (
2.77
) one obtains

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l})+\diver_{x}(\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l})\bm{v}^{l})-\varepsilon_{4}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l}):(\mathbb{F}^{l}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l,T}+\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{l,T})\\
&+\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{l}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{l}}\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}^{l}):((\mathbb{B}^{l})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{l})=2(\mathbb{B}^{l}-\mathbb{I}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{l}|)\frac{(\theta^{l}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{l}}.\end{split}

(3.41)

in the sense of operators on
C^{1}([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega})
, for
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
defined as in (
3.9
). Now, the only obstacle to copy verbatum the argument in Subsection
2.2.1
are the properties (
2.77
) and (
2.80
). Note that the terms without
\varepsilon_{4}
can be treated the same way as in Subsection
2.2.1
, that is they are
L^{1}
functions, which space embeds compactly into
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
. In fact, the term with
\varepsilon_{4}
is also proper, as at this point we keep
\varepsilon_{4}
fixed, but since in the next step we shall converge with
\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}
, let us show the exact bounds at this point, so later it will be clear that those are also independent of
\varepsilon_{4}
. Hence, let us show that
\varepsilon_{4}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})}{\theta^{l}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l}):(\mathbb{F}^{l}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l,T}+\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{l,T})
defines a compact sequence in
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
, where we choose the non-zero trace Sobolev space to keep in mind the boundary conditions. The term in (
3.41
) can be treated the same way. Fix
\varphi\in W^{1,5}(Q_{T})
with
\|\varphi\|_{W^{1,5}}\leq 1
. Then,

\begin{split}&-\left\langle\varepsilon_{4}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})}{\theta^{l}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l}):(\mathbb{F}^{l}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l,T}+\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{l,T}),\varphi\right\rangle_{((W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*},W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))}\\
&=2\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\varphi\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})}{\theta^{l}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l}):(\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l,T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\varphi\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})}{\theta^{l}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\det\mathbb{F}^{l}>\varepsilon_{2}\}}|(\mathbb{B}^{l})^{-1}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{B}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\varphi\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})-\theta^{l}g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})}{(\theta^{l})^{2}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l}):(\mathbb{F}^{l}(\nabla_{x}\theta^{l}\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l,T})+(\nabla_{x}\theta^{l}\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l})\mathbb{F}^{l,T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})}{\theta^{l}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l}):(\mathbb{F}^{l}(\nabla_{x}\varphi\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l,T})+(\nabla_{x}\varphi\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l})\mathbb{F}^{l,T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&=:I^{l}_{1}+I^{l}_{2}+I^{l}_{3}+I^{l}_{4}.\end{split}

(3.42)

Here, we bound

\begin{split}|I^{l}_{1}|\leq 2\left\|\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})}{\theta^{l}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l})\right\|_{\infty}\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}|\varphi||\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\leq C(\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3})\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,\end{split}

(3.43)

and

\begin{split}|I^{l}_{2}|\leq\left\|\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})}{\theta^{l}}(\mathbb{B}^{l})^{-2}\right\|_{\infty}\|(\mathbb{F}^{l})^{2}\|_{\infty}\,\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}|\varphi||\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\leq C(\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3})\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,\end{split}

(3.44)

as well as using Hölder’s inequality

\begin{split}|I^{l}_{3}|&\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_{4}}\|(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})-\theta^{l}g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l}))\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l})\|_{\infty}\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\left\|\frac{\nabla_{x}\theta^{l}}{(\theta^{l})^{2}}\right\|_{2}\|\sqrt{\varepsilon_{4}}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\|_{2}\\
&\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_{4}}C(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6},K)\left(\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+1\right)\|\sqrt{\varepsilon_{4}}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\|_{2},\end{split}

(3.45)

and

\begin{split}|I^{l}_{4}|\leq 2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{4}}\left\|\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})}{\theta^{l}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l})\right\|_{\infty}\|\mathbb{F}^{l}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla_{x}\varphi\|_{2}\|\sqrt{\varepsilon_{4}}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_{4}}C(\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3})\|\sqrt{\varepsilon_{4}}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\|_{2}\end{split}

(3.46)

which means that
I^{l}_{1}
,
I^{l}_{2}
,
I^{l}_{3}
, and
I^{l}_{4}
are operators defined by
L^{1}
functions, which are uniformly bounded in
L^{1}
with respect to the
l
. Hence, by Sobolev embeddings, we can deduce that there exists
T\in(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
such that (up to the subsequence)

I^{l}_{1}+I^{l}_{2}+I^{l}_{3}+I^{l}_{4}\rightarrow(T,\varphi)_{((W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*},W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))}\text{, as }l\to+\infty.

Thus, indeed
\varepsilon_{4}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})}{\theta^{l}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{l}):(\mathbb{F}^{l}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l,T}+\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{l,T})
defines a compact sequence in
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
, which is what we wanted to show. Here, we may finally follow Subsection
2.2.1
to deduce

\displaystyle\theta^{l}\rightarrow\theta\quad\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T},

which combined with (
3.39
), and our comments below (
3.39
) gives us

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta^{l}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{q}(0,T;(W^{1,q}(\Omega))^{*}),q>5

\displaystyle\theta^{l}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<5/3,

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\theta^{l}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\nabla_{x}\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<5/4,

\displaystyle\theta^{l}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T},

\displaystyle(\theta^{l})^{\frac{2-\lambda}{2}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\theta^{\frac{2-\lambda}{2}},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)),\,\lambda\in(0,1).

Moreover, analysing (
3.40
), one can obtain similarly to (
2.45
) and (
2.47
) we can deduce

\displaystyle\ln\theta^{l}\rightharpoonup\ln\theta,\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)).

Now, from (
3.36
) we can see that
e^{l}
inherits its regularity from
\theta^{l}
and
\mathbb{F}^{l}
. One obtains

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e^{l}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{q}(0,T;(W^{1,q}(\Omega))^{*}),q>5

\displaystyle e^{l}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<5/3,

\displaystyle e^{l}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

At last, to converge in equations (
3.33
)–(
3.35
) we need to show that

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nu(\theta^{l})|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\rightarrow\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nu(\theta)|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s,\text{ as }l\to+\infty.

We shall employ the classical monotonicity method. Using the proven convergences for variables, we can converge with (
3.33
) to obtain

\partial_{t}\bm{v}+\diver_{x}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\bm{v}|^{2})\bm{v}\otimes\bm{v})-\diver_{x}\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v})=0,

in the sense of distributions. Testing the equation above by
\bm{v}
we get the energy equality in the limiting equation

\begin{split}\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}(t)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}(0)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}|\bm{v}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}S(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(3.47)

Now, we compare it to the limit of (
3.37
). We have

\begin{split}&\limsup_{l\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nu(\theta^{l})|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=\limsup_{l\to+\infty}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}^{l}(0)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}^{l}(t)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right)\\
&\qquad-\liminf_{l\to+\infty}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}|\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}S(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}2\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{l}|)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})\mathbb{F}^{l}\,\mathbb{F}^{l,T}\frac{(\theta^{l}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{l}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\right)\end{split}

(3.48)

By weak lower-semi continuity of norms, and convergence of initial datum we deduce

\begin{split}\limsup_{l\to+\infty}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}^{l}(0)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}^{l}(t)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right)\leq\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}(0)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}(t)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

Using the trace theorem, and again the lower semi-continuity of norms

\displaystyle-\liminf_{l\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}|\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}S(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\leq-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}|\bm{v}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}S(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s.

At last, by the strong convergence of
\mathbb{F}^{l}
, the almost everywhere convergence of
\theta^{l}
, the Vitali’s convergence theorem, and the weak convergence of
\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}
we imply

\begin{split}\lim_{l\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}2\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{l}|)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l})\mathbb{F}^{l}\,\mathbb{F}^{l,T}\frac{(\theta^{l}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{l}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}2\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

Combining the above with (
3.48
) we get

\begin{split}&\limsup_{l\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nu(\theta^{l})|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\leq\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}(0)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}|\bm{v}(t)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}|\bm{v}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}S(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}2\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nu(\theta)|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

Here, we note that due to the boundedness of
\nu
from (
1.15
), almost everywhere convergence of
\theta^{l}
and the weak convergence of
\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}
we can obtain

\sqrt{\nu(\theta^{l})}\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}\rightharpoonup\sqrt{\nu(\theta)}\mathbb{D}\bm{v}\text{, weakly in }L^{2}(Q_{T}),

thus by weak lower-semicontinuity of norms

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nu(\theta)|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\leq\liminf_{l\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nu(\theta^{l})|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s.

Therefore, we have shown what we needed, i.e.

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nu(\theta)|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=\lim_{l\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nu(\theta^{l})|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s.

Using all our proven convergences, we may converge from (
3.33
)-(
3.35
) to the system

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}+\diver_{x}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\bm{v}|^{2})\bm{v}\otimes\bm{v})-\diver_{x}\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v})=0,

(3.49)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}+\Diver_{x}(\mathbb{F}\otimes\bm{v})-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\mathbb{F}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}-\varepsilon_{4}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}+\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}-\mathbb{F})=0,

(3.50)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e+\diver_{x}(e\bm{v})-\diver_{x}(\kappa(\theta)\nabla_{x}\theta)-\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}=0,

(3.51)

where (
3.49
)–(
3.51
) are to be understood in the weak sense in the sense of an operator on a predual space to the time derivative. Note that from (
3.36
) we keep

\begin{split}e(t,x)=\theta(t,x)+(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x))-\theta(t,x)g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x)))\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}),\end{split}

(3.52)

for
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
defined as in (
3.9
).

Beforem moving onto the next step, let us show one more convergence which will help us identify the operator
T
. Consider the equation (
3.34
) tested by
\mathbb{F}^{l}
, which gives as energy equality

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau=-\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}^{l}(t,x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}^{l}_{0}(x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{l}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{l}:\mathbb{F}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{l,T}\frac{(\theta^{l}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{l}}-\frac{\tau(\theta^{l})}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{l}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{l}}(|\mathbb{F}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{l,T}|^{2}-|\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau,\end{split}

(3.53)

as well as the equation (
3.50
) tested by
\mathbb{F}
which provides

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau=-\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}(t,x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}_{0}(x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{l}:\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}-\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(|\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|^{2}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau.\end{split}

(3.54)

Now using the already established convergences for
\mathbb{F}^{l}
,
\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{l}
and
\theta^{l}
we can deduce

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{l}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{l}&:\mathbb{F}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{l,T}\frac{(\theta^{l}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{l}}-\frac{\tau(\theta^{l})}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{l}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{l}}(|\mathbb{F}^{l}\mathbb{F}^{l,T}|^{2}-|\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau\\
&\rightarrow\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}:\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}-\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(|\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|^{2}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau.\end{split}

Thus, applying as well weak lower semicontinuity of norms we obtain

\begin{split}&\limsup_{l\to+\infty}\left(\varepsilon_{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau\right)\leq-\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}(t,x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}_{0}(x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}:\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}-\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(|\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|^{2}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau\\
&=\varepsilon_{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau.\end{split}

Since by weak lower semicontinuity

\liminf_{l\to+\infty}\left(\varepsilon_{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau\right)\geq\varepsilon_{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau,

this implies

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to+\infty}\left(\varepsilon_{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{l}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau\right)=\varepsilon_{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau,

(3.55)

and in consequence

\mathbb{F}^{l}\rightarrow\mathbb{F},\text{ strongly in }L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3}).

Of a particular attention is a fact, that we can now identify the operator
T
defined under (
3.46
). Looking at the definitions of
I^{l}_{i}
in (
3.42
) the now established strong convergence of the gradient of
\mathbb{F}^{l}
allows us to see that

\displaystyle I_{1}^{l}\rightarrow 2\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\varphi\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)}{\theta}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}):(\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle I_{2}^{l}\rightarrow\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\varphi\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)}{\theta}\mathbf{1}_{\{\det\mathbb{F}>\varepsilon_{2}\}}|(\mathbb{B})^{-1}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{B}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle I_{3}^{l}\rightarrow\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\varphi\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)-\theta^{l}g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)}{(\theta)^{2}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}):(\mathbb{F}(\nabla_{x}\theta\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{T})+(\nabla_{x}\theta\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F})\mathbb{F}^{T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle I_{4}^{l}\rightarrow\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)}{\theta}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}):(\mathbb{F}(\nabla_{x}\varphi\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{T})+(\nabla_{x}\varphi\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F})\mathbb{F}^{T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

hence

\begin{split}T(\varphi)&=2\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\varphi\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)}{\theta}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}):(\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\quad+\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\varphi\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)}{\theta}\mathbf{1}_{\{\det\mathbb{F}>\varepsilon_{2}\}}|(\mathbb{B})^{-1}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{B}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\quad+\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\varphi\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)-\theta^{l}g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)}{(\theta)^{2}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}):(\mathbb{F}(\nabla_{x}\theta\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{T})+(\nabla_{x}\theta\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F})\mathbb{F}^{T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\quad+\int_{Q_{T}}\varepsilon_{4}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)}{\theta}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}):(\mathbb{F}(\nabla_{x}\varphi\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{T})+(\nabla_{x}\varphi\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F})\mathbb{F}^{T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t.\end{split}

(3.56)

Step 6: convergence with
\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}
.
Similarly as in the previous step

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}_{\bm{n},\diver}(\Omega))^{*}),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}_{\bm{n},\diver}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<10/3,

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

Again, one can verify that (
3.50
) can be tested by
\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
, which means that we again obtain the bounds on
\{\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\}_{\varepsilon_{4}>0}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;(L^{2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})
, as well as the bound on
\{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{4}}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\}_{\varepsilon_{4}>0}
in
(L^{2}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3\times 3}
. Repeating the argument at the beginning of Step 4, we imply that
\{\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\}_{\varepsilon_{4}>0}
is bounded in
(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
, see also comments under (
3.17
). A priori this is not enough to converge strongly with the sequence
\{\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\}_{\varepsilon_{4}>0}
, and we shall utilize the technique presented in Subsection
2.2.2
. But before that, we need to establish compactness of the temperature sequence
\{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}\}_{\varepsilon_{4}>0}
. To do so, note that by weak lower semicontinuity of an
L^{2}
norm of
\nabla_{x}(\theta^{1-\lambda/2})
and the established convergence (
3.55
) we can deduce from (
3.39
)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})^{\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\leq C(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6},K,\lambda)\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+C(\lambda,K,\varepsilon_{3}),\qquad\lambda\in(1,2),

(3.57)

and following the argument under (
2.37
) we can deduce that
\{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}\}_{\varepsilon_{4}>0}
is bounded in
L^{q}(Q_{T})
for
q<5/3
and
\{\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}\}_{\varepsilon_{4}>0}
is bounded in
L^{q}(Q_{T})
for
q<5/4
. With this, we wish to follow the argument in Subsection
2.2.1
, which means that we need the equations for the entropy
\eta^{\varepsilon_{4}}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
. At this point of the approximation, the functions
1/\theta
is no longer a proper test function as the duality between
\partial_{t}e^{\varepsilon_{4}}
and
1/\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}
is "not correct". Thus, we will converge in some sense in the equality (
3.40
). Using our established convergences it is easy to see that for the first three terms of the left-hand side of (
3.40
)

\partial_{t}\eta^{l,\varepsilon_{4}}+\diver_{x}(\eta^{l,\varepsilon_{4}}\bm{v}^{l,\varepsilon_{4}})-\diver_{x}\left(\kappa\frac{\nabla_{x}\theta^{l,\varepsilon_{4}}}{\theta^{l,\varepsilon_{4}}}\right)\overset{*}{\rightharpoonup}\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon_{4}}+\diver_{x}(\eta^{\varepsilon_{4}}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}})-\diver_{x}\left(\kappa\frac{\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\right),

as
l\to+\infty
in
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
. For the fourth term we already know that it converges in the sense of operators to some
T^{\varepsilon_{4}}
defined below (
3.46
), and identified in (
3.56
). Moreover, using the convergence (
3.55
), this operator satisfies a bound

\|T^{\varepsilon_{4}}\|_{(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}}\\
\leq C(\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3})\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{4}}C(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6},K)\left(\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+1\right)\|\sqrt{\varepsilon_{4}}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\|_{2},

(3.58)

which we will show soon converges to
0
as
\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}
, making sure we get rid of this term after this step. For the right-hand side of (
3.40
) we use the fact that all three terms are bounded in
L^{1}
, hence we can define a measure
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{+}(Q_{T})
by

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle:=\text{weak}^{*}\lim\left(\frac{\kappa|\nabla_{x}\theta^{l,\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{l,\varepsilon_{4}})^{2}}-\frac{\kappa|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\text{weak}^{*}\lim\left(\frac{2\nu|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}}{\theta^{l,\varepsilon_{4}}}-\frac{2\nu|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\text{weak}^{*}\lim\Bigg(\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{l,\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{l,\varepsilon_{4}}}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{l,\varepsilon_{4}})\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}^{l,\varepsilon_{4}}):((\mathbb{B}^{l,\varepsilon_{4}})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{l,\varepsilon_{4}})}{\theta^{l,\varepsilon_{4}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}}):((\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}})}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\Bigg),

where this measure is positive due to the weak lower semicontinuity of norms. Thus, after the convergence with
l\to+\infty
in (
3.40
) we deduce

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon_{4}}+\diver_{x}(\eta^{\varepsilon_{4}}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}})-\diver_{x}\left(\kappa\frac{\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\right)-T^{\varepsilon_{4}}\\
&=\frac{\kappa|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})^{2}}+\frac{2\nu|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}+\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}}):((\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}})}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}+\mu^{\varepsilon_{4}},\end{split}

(3.59)

in the sense of operators in
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
, for
\eta^{\varepsilon_{4}}=\ln\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}})
. The equation for
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
still has proper duality, therefore following the procedure under (
2.77
) one obtains

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}})+\diver_{x}(\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}})\bm{v}_{\varepsilon_{4}})-\varepsilon_{4}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}}):(\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}+\Delta_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T})\\
&+\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}}):((\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}})=2(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\mathbb{I}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{l}_{\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}.\end{split}

(3.60)

in the sense of operators on
C^{1}([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega})
, for
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
defined as in (
3.9
). Now, we can copy verbatum the argument in Subsection
2.2.1
as in the previous step. Since (
3.59
) can be tested by
1
, we can deduce that the right-hand side is bounded in total variation norm, hence, we may embed it compactly into
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
. Since
T^{\varepsilon_{4}}
is defined by (
3.42
) it is also defined via uniformly bounded
L^{1}
functions, and defines a compact sequence in
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
. The term in (
3.60
) can be treated the same way. Thus, we may follow Subsection
2.2.1
to deduce

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}\rightarrow\theta\quad\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T},

which combined with (
3.57
), and our comments below (
3.57
) gives us

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{q}(0,T;(W^{1,q}(\Omega))^{*}),q>5

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<5/3,

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\nabla_{x}\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<5/4,

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T},

\displaystyle(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})^{\frac{2-\lambda}{2}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\theta^{\frac{2-\lambda}{2}},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)),\,\lambda\in(0,1).

Moreover, analysing (
3.59
), one can obtain similarly to (
2.45
) and (
2.47
) the convergence

\displaystyle\ln\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}\rightharpoonup\ln\theta,\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)).

Having those convergences we can come back to the problem of strong convergence of
\{\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\}_{\varepsilon_{4}>0}
. At this point we can deduce that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{2}(0,T;((W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})^{*}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3},

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{4}\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup 0,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }(L^{2}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}.

Thus, we can converge in (
3.34
) to obtain

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}+\Diver_{x}(\mathbb{F}\otimes\bm{v})-\overline{\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\,\mathbb{F}}\,\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}+\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\left(\overline{\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}}-\overline{\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}}\right)=0,\end{split}

(3.61)

where, in accordance to the convention (
2.64
) the function
\overline{\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\,\mathbb{F}}
is a weak limit of a function
\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\,\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
in
L^{2}(Q_{T})
, and
\overline{\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}}
is a weak* limit of the function
\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\,\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}\,\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
in
(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
; analogously we define
\overline{\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{F}}
. As in Subsection
2.2.2
, one wants to compare (
3.50
) tested by
\varphi\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
and (
3.61
) tested by
\varphi\mathbb{F}
, for any non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}((-\infty,T)\times\Omega)
(note that at this point we do not need the function
G_{m}
defined in (
2.94
), because the velocity has high enough regularity). Moreover, testing (
3.50
) by
\varphi\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
gives us two additional terms

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,\qquad\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}(\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi)\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

first of which is non-negative, and second of which disappears as
\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}
, hence, they are invisible when it comes to inequalities. With this in mind, one can easily verify that in this case we obtain the following analogue of (
2.95
) valid for all non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}((-\infty,T)\times\Omega)
:

\begin{split}-&\int_{Q_{T}}\left(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2}\right)\partial_{t}\varphi-\int_{Q_{T}}\left(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2}\right)\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi\\
&\qquad\leq\int_{Q_{T}}\left(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2}\right)\varphi-\ell_{1}+2\ell_{2},\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\ell_{1}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{Q_{T}}\tau(\theta)\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\left(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}|^{2}-\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}:\mathbb{F}\right)\varphi,

\displaystyle\ell_{2}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{Q_{T}}\left(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\,\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}:\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}:\mathbb{F}\right)\varphi.

We will verify, that
\ell_{1}
is a sum of two integrals, one of which is non-negative, and the second one which satisfies an inequality similar to the one for
\ell_{2}
below, while
\ell_{2}
satisfies

\ell_{2}\leq\int_{Q_{T}}\tilde{L}\left(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2}\right)\varphi

for all non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}((-\infty,T)\times\Omega)
,
and some
\tilde{L}
– an
L^{2}(Q_{T})
function specified below, which will allow us to follow Subsection
2.2.2
and conclude that
\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
converges strongly in
(L^{2}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
to
\mathbb{F}
. For
\ell_{1}
we may write

\begin{split}&\int_{Q_{T}}\tau(\theta)\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\left(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}|^{2}-\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}:\mathbb{F}\right)\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&=\int_{Q_{T}}\tau(\theta)(\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}):(\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\mathbb{F})\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{Q_{T}}\tau(\theta)\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}:(\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\mathbb{F})\left(\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}}-\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\right)\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{Q_{T}}\tau(\theta)\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}:(\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\mathbb{F})\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&=:I_{1}^{\varepsilon_{4}}+I_{2}^{\varepsilon_{4}}+I_{3}^{\varepsilon_{4}}.\end{split}

The fact that
I_{1}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\geq 0
follows from the monotonicity of a function
\mathbb{F}\mapsto\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}\mathbb{F}
(see for example
[
13
, Lemma 4.2]
). Let us move into handling
I_{2}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
. Notice that since
\{\mathbb{F}\}_{\varepsilon_{4}}
is bounded in
(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
, there exists a
C>0
for which
\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
is contained in the set
\{F\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}:|F|\leq C\}
. Now, we may calculate

\partial\frac{(\det F-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det F}=\mathbf{1}_{\{\det F>\varepsilon_{5}\}}\frac{\varepsilon_{5}}{\det F}F^{-T}:\partial F,

and on the set
\{F\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}:|F|\leq C\}

\left|\mathbf{1}_{\{\det F>\varepsilon_{5}\}}\frac{\varepsilon_{5}}{\det F}F^{-T}\right|\leq 3\frac{|F|^{2}}{\det F}\leq\frac{3C^{2}}{\varepsilon_{5}}.

Hence,

F\mapsto\frac{(\det F-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det F}

is Lipschitz on this set and one can bound

\begin{split}|I_{2}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|\leq\frac{3K}{\varepsilon_{5}}C^{5}\int_{Q_{T}}|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\mathbb{F}|^{2}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\rightarrow\frac{3K}{\varepsilon_{5}}C^{5}\int_{Q_{T}}(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,\end{split}

as
\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}
. For the term
I_{3}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
we can deduce from the weak* convergence of
\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
to
\mathbb{F}
in
(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
that

I_{3}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\rightarrow 0^{+},\quad\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}.

To handle
\ell_{2}
let us first recall, that analogously to the arguments below (
2.103
) and (
2.104
) we can split the velocity into two parts
\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}=\bm{v}_{1}^{\varepsilon_{4}}+\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}_{2}
, where

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{1}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\bm{v}_{1}

\displaystyle\mbox{weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W_{\bm{n},\diver}^{1,2}),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{1}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{1}

\displaystyle\mbox{weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;(W_{\bm{n},\diver}^{1,2})^{*}),

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{1}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\to\bm{v}_{1}\quad

\displaystyle\mbox{strongly in }(L^{\frac{10}{3})}(Q_{T}))^{3},

and

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{2}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\bm{v}_{2}

\displaystyle\mbox{weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W_{\bm{n},\diver}^{1,2}),

\displaystyle\nabla\bm{v}_{2}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\to\nabla\bm{v}_{2}

\displaystyle\mbox{strongly in }(L^{2)}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}.

Moreover, by Proposition
1.3

\displaystyle\left(2\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\,\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}+\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}_{1}\right):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\rightharpoonup\left(2\overline{\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}}+\mathbb{D}\bm{v}_{1}\right):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}_{1}\text{, in a biting sense in }L^{1}(Q_{T}),

see also Proposition
1.2
for the definition of the biting convergence. Let us denote by
E_{j}
the sequence of sets for which
|Q_{T}\setminus E_{j}|\rightarrow 0
as
j\to+\infty
, and

\displaystyle\left(2\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\,\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}+\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}_{1}\right):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\rightharpoonup\left(2\overline{\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}}+\mathbb{D}\bm{v}_{1}\right):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}_{1}\text{, weakly in }L^{1}(E_{j}).

(3.62)

Furthermore, following (
2.119
) let
\mathcal{E}_{k}
be the sequence of sets for which
|Q_{T}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{k}|\rightarrow 0
and

\frac{1}{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})}\rightarrow\frac{1}{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)},\text{ strongly in }L^{\infty}(\mathcal{E}_{k})

Note that, due to (
3.62
)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\left(2\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\,\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-2\overline{\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}}:\mathbb{D}\bm{v}\right)\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}_{1}-\mathbb{D}\bm{v}|^{2}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t.

(3.63)

Having this, we may write

\begin{split}&\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\left(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}:\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}:\mathbb{F}\right)\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&=\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\left(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}:\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\,\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}:\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\right)\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\mathbb{F})\nabla_{x}\bm{v}:(\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\mathbb{F})\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad-\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\mathbb{F})(\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\nabla_{x}\bm{v}):\mathbb{F}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\left(\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\mathbb{F}:\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\mathbb{F}:\mathbb{F}\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&=:J_{1}^{\varepsilon_{4}}+J_{2}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-J_{3}^{\varepsilon_{4}}+J_{4}^{\varepsilon_{4}}.\end{split}

Let us treat all the terms separately. For
J_{1}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
we can write by (
3.63
) and the strong convergence of the gradient of
\bm{v}_{2}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\begin{split}&\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\left(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}:\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\,\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}:\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\right)\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\leq-\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\frac{|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}_{1}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\mathbb{D}\bm{v}_{1}|^{2}}{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+\frac{4}{\varepsilon_{3}^{2}}\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}|\nabla_{x}\bm{v}_{2}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\nabla_{x}\bm{v}_{2}|\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\leq-\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\frac{|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}_{1}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\mathbb{D}\bm{v}_{1}|^{2}}{R}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,\end{split}

where
R:=\sup_{\theta>0}g(\theta)>0
. When it comes to
J_{4}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
we can see that due to the almost everywhere convergence of
\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}
and the Vitali’s convergence theorem

\displaystyle\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}\to\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta},\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,\,q<+\infty,

hence using weak convergence of
\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
and
\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}J_{4}^{\varepsilon_{4}}=0.

Before moving to
J_{2}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
and
J_{3}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
notice that
F\to\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|F|)
can be chosen in such a way that
0\leq|\partial\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|F|)|\leq 2\varepsilon_{3}
. Hence, the function

F\mapsto\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|F|)F

is Lipschitz with (without a loss of generality) a Lipschitz constant smaller than
1
, and we may write

\begin{split}&\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}J_{2}^{\varepsilon_{4}}=\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\mathbb{F})\nabla_{x}\bm{v}:(\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\mathbb{F})\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\leq\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla_{x}\bm{v}|(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,\end{split}

Moving further, for
J_{3}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
using Young’s inequality, localized version of the Korn’s inequality for sequences (see for example
[
14
]
in the Appendix), and strong convergence of
\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}_{2}
in
L^{1}(Q_{T})
, we deduce

\begin{split}&\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}J_{3}^{\varepsilon_{4}}=\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\mathbb{F})(\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\nabla_{x}\bm{v}):\mathbb{F}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad\leq\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}|\mathbb{F}||\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\mathbb{F}||\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}_{1}-\nabla_{x}\bm{v}_{1}|\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad\leq C(R)\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}|\mathbb{F}|^{2}(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\int_{E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}}\frac{|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}_{1}-\mathbb{D}\bm{v}_{1}|^{2}}{2R}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\end{split}

Adding everything together we obtain

\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}J_{1}^{\varepsilon_{4}}+J_{2}^{\varepsilon_{4}}+J_{3}^{\varepsilon_{4}}+J_{4}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\leq\int_{Q_{T}}(|\nabla_{x}\bm{v}|+C(R)|\mathbb{F}|^{2})(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t.

All that is left to do is consider the integral over the set
|Q_{T}\setminus(E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k})|
. Here, we easily see by Hölder’s inequality

\begin{split}&\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\left|\int_{Q_{T}\setminus(E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k})}\left(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}:\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}:\mathbb{F}\right)\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\right|\\
&\qquad\leq\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\frac{8}{\varepsilon^{2}_{3}}\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\int_{Q_{T}\setminus(E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k})}|\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad\leq C(K,\varepsilon_{3})\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\sqrt{|Q_{T}\setminus(E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k})|}.\end{split}

Since
|Q_{T}\setminus(E_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k})|\to 0
as
j,k\to+\infty
, we finally obtain the needed

\ell_{2}\leq\int_{Q_{T}}(|\nabla_{x}\bm{v}|+C(R)|\mathbb{F}|^{2})(\overline{|\mathbb{F}|^{2}}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t.

Thus, by Subsection
2.2.2

\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\rightarrow\mathbb{F},\text{ strongly in }L^{2}(Q_{T}),

which by interpolation implies

\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\rightarrow\mathbb{F},\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<+\infty.

Now, from (
3.52
) we can see that
e^{\varepsilon_{4}}
inherits its regularity from
\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}
and
\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
. One obtains

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{q}(0,T;(W^{1,q}(\Omega))^{*}),q>5

\displaystyle e^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<5/3,

\displaystyle e^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

To keep the equality in the internal energy equation one needs to now show that
\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
converges strongly in
L^{2}(Q_{T})
. As we have already shown strong convergence of
\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
in
L^{2}(Q_{T})
one can repeat the argument given in the previous step (see the discussion under (
3.47
)). All of the convergences are enough to go from (
3.49
)–(
3.51
) to

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}+\diver_{x}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\bm{v}|^{2})\bm{v}\otimes\bm{v})-\diver_{x}\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v})=0,

(3.64)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}+\Diver_{x}(\mathbb{F}\otimes\bm{v})-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\,\mathbb{F}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}+\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}-\mathbb{F})=0,

(3.65)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e+\diver_{x}(e\bm{v})-\diver_{x}(\kappa(\theta)\nabla_{x}\theta)-\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}=0,

(3.66)

where (
3.64
)–(
3.66
) are to be understood in the weak sense in the sense of an operator on a predual space to the time derivative. Note that from (
3.52
) we keep

\begin{split}e(t,x)=\theta(t,x)+(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x))-\theta(t,x)g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x)))\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}),\end{split}

(3.67)

for
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
defined as in (
3.9
).

Here, let us get rid of the operator
T^{\varepsilon_{4}}
from the entropy equality (
3.59
). We will show that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\left(\varepsilon_{4}\int_{Q_{T}}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\right)=0,

(3.68)

which by (
3.58
) will imply
T^{\varepsilon_{4}}\to 0
strongly in
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
. Consider the equation (
3.50
) tested by
\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
, which gives as energy equality

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau=-\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}(t,x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}_{0}(x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}:\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}-\frac{\tau(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}|^{2}-|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau,\end{split}

(3.69)

as well as the equation (
3.65
) tested by
\mathbb{F}
which provides

\begin{split}&0=-\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}(t,x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}_{0}(x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{l}:\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}-\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(|\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|^{2}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau.\end{split}

(3.70)

Note, that the testing can be easily justified for the term
\Diver(F\otimes v)
, even though the gradient of
\mathbb{F}
no longer exists, via the Commutator lemma (cf.
[
13
, Lemma 4.1]
or
[
19
, Lemma II.1]
), since
\mathbb{F}\in(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
, and
\bm{v}\in L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega))
. Now using the already established convergences for
\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
,
\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}
and
\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}
we can deduce

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{4}}&:\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}}-\frac{\tau(\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}})}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4},T}|^{2}-|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau\\
&\rightarrow\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}:\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}-\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(|\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|^{2}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau.\end{split}

Thus, applying as well weak lower semicontinuity of norms we obtain

\begin{split}&\limsup_{\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}}\left(\varepsilon_{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{4}}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau\right)\leq-\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}(t,x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}_{0}(x)|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}:\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}-\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(|\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|^{2}-|\mathbb{F}|^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau\\
&=0,\end{split}

which is what we wanted.

For further reference, we mention that one can converge with
l\to+\infty
and
\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}
in lambda entropy equality (
3.38
) analogously as for the standard entropy (
3.40
), and obtain

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\eta_{\lambda}-\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}((\mathbb{B})^{2}-\mathbb{B}):\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B})(h_{\lambda}(\theta)-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)(\theta)^{\lambda})\\
&\qquad+2(\mathbb{B}-\mathbb{I}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}(h_{\lambda}(\theta)-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)(\theta)^{\lambda})\\
&=(1-\lambda)\kappa\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{l}|^{2}}{(\theta)^{2-\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+2\nu\frac{|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}|^{2}}{(\theta)^{2-\lambda}}+\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta)((\mathbb{B})^{2}-\mathbb{B}):\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B})}{(\theta)^{1-\lambda}}+\nu_{\lambda},\end{split}

(3.71)

as an operator in
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
, for
\nu_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{M}^{+}(Q_{T})
.

Step 7:
\det\mathbb{F}\geq\varepsilon_{5}
, and
\det\mathbb{F}>0
uniformly.

Let

\mathbb{G}_{\delta}:=\mathbb{F}^{-T}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\delta)_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}.

Note that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{\delta}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{C\|\mathbb{F}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\delta)_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\leq\frac{C\|\mathbb{F}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\delta},

hence
\mathbb{G}_{\delta}\in(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
. Now, we would like to test (
3.65
) by
\mathbb{G}_{\delta}
, which requires mollification. As the argument is standard, we again skip it for the sake of brevity, and only mention that to treat the term with the divergence one should utilize the Commutator lemma
[
13
, Lemma 4.1]
,
[
19
, Lemma II.1]
(see also
[
13
, Section 6.5]
for the full description of the method for the interested readers). Before writing down the full expression after the testing, let us denote the primitive function of
s\mapsto\frac{(s-\delta)_{+}}{s^{2}}
as

\displaystyle S_{\delta}(s):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\ln(s)+\frac{\delta}{s},\text{ whenever }s>\delta,\\
\ln\delta+1,\text{ otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Then, using the identity (cf.
[
4
, Lemma A.3]
)
\partial\ln(\det\mathbb{F})=\partial\mathbb{F}:\mathbb{F}^{-T}
and the fact that
\diver_{x}\bm{v}=0
, one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(S_{\delta}(\det\mathbb{F}(t))-S_{\delta}(\det\mathbb{F}_{0}))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\,\mathbb{F}:\mathbb{G}_{\delta}+\frac{\tau}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}-\mathbb{F}):\mathbb{G}_{\delta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=0.

(3.72)

Due to the fact that
\bm{v}
is divergence free, we may write

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\,\mathbb{F}:\mathbb{G}_{\delta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\delta)_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{F}^{-1}\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\mathbb{F})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\delta)_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\mathrm{tr}(\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\,\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{-1})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=0.\end{split}

(3.73)

Thus, we have from (
3
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(S_{\delta}(\det\mathbb{F}(t))-S_{\delta}(\det\mathbb{F}_{0}))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\tau}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\delta)_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(|\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}|-3)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=0,

which implies

\begin{split}\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\left|\int_{\Omega}S_{\delta}(\det\mathbb{F}(t))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq\int_{\Omega}1+|\ln\det\mathbb{F}_{0}|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}|^{2}+3\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t.\end{split}

(3.74)

Since the right-hand side is bounded uniformly with respect to
\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}
, and

\displaystyle S_{\delta}(s)\rightarrow S(s):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\ln(s),\text{ whenever }s>0,\\
-\infty,\text{ otherwise},\end{array}\right.

the inequality above implies, that
\det\mathbb{F}>0
a.e. in
Q_{T}
, which in principle ends the proof of a second part of our step. We will now show the bound on
\|\ln\det\mathbb{F}\|_{1}
, which will be useful for keeping this property throughout the limiting procedures, as by Fatou’s lemma it will be kept whenever we have an almost everywhere convergent sequence. From (
3.74
), by Fatou’s lemma

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\left|\int_{\Omega}\ln\det\mathbb{F}(t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq\int_{\Omega}1+|\ln\det\mathbb{F}_{0}|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}|^{2}+3\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

hence

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(\ln\det\mathbb{F})_{-}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(\ln\det\mathbb{F})_{+}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+T\int_{\Omega}1+|\ln\det\mathbb{F}_{0}|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{F}|^{2}+3\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

which is enough to bound the negative part. As for the positive one, we can simply write using the known inequality
\ln\det(\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T})\leq\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T})-3\leq|\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|-3

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(\ln\det\mathbb{F})_{+}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t=\int_{\{\det\mathbb{F}>1\}}\ln\det\mathbb{F}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\{\det\mathbb{F}>1\}}\ln(\det\mathbb{F})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\{\det\mathbb{F}>1\}}\ln(\det\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\{\det\mathbb{F}>1\}}|\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}|-3\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{Q_{T}}|\mathbb{F}|^{2}+3\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t.\end{split}

In the end

\begin{split}\|\ln\det\mathbb{F}\|_{1}=\int_{Q_{T}}(\ln\det\mathbb{F})_{+}+(\ln\det\mathbb{F})_{-}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\leq C(|Q_{T}|,\|\mathbb{F}\|_{2},\|\ln\det\mathbb{F}_{0}\|_{1}).\end{split}

(3.75)

We can move to proving the non-uniform bound from below on
\det\mathbb{F}
. Since we already know that
\det\mathbb{F}>0
, then
\mathbb{F}^{-1}
is well defined, and we can denote

\mathbb{H}:=-\mathbb{F}^{-T}\det\mathbb{F}(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{-}.

Using standard properties of the determinant, we can bound

\displaystyle(\det\mathbb{F})^{2}=\det(\mathbb{F}^{T}\mathbb{F})\leq(\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{F}^{T}\mathbb{F}))^{3}=|\mathbb{F}|^{3},

which implies

\displaystyle\|\det\mathbb{F}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathbb{F}\|_{\infty}^{3/2}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{H}\|_{\infty}\leq C\|\mathbb{F}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5}\|_{\infty}\leq C(\|\mathbb{F}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\|\mathbb{F}\|_{\infty}^{5/2}),

and in consequence
\mathbb{H}\in(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
. Similarly as above we now test (
3.65
) by
\mathbb{H}
, which necessitates the use of mollification, where the procedure is the same as for
\mathbb{G}_{\delta}
above. One can observe that
\partial(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})^{2}_{-}=-\partial\mathbb{F}:\mathbb{F}^{-T}\det\mathbb{F}(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{-}
, thus after testing, and using
\diver_{x}\bm{v}=0
, we get similarly as before

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}((\det\mathbb{F}(t)-\varepsilon_{5})^{2}_{-}-(\det\mathbb{F}_{0}-\varepsilon_{5})^{2}_{-})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\,\mathbb{F}:\mathbb{H}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s

(3.76)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\tau}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}-\mathbb{F}):\mathbb{H}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=0.

(3.77)

By an analogous procedure as in (
3.73
)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\,\mathbb{F}:\mathbb{H}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=0,

and using the fact that

(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{-}(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}=0,

we may deduce

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\tau}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}-\mathbb{F}):\mathbb{H}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=0.

Combining this with (
3.76
) we get

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|(\det\mathbb{F}(t)-\varepsilon_{5})_{-}\|_{2}\leq\|(\det\mathbb{F}_{0}-\varepsilon_{5})_{-}\|_{2}.

Since (briefly we introduce back our indexes)

\displaystyle\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{5}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\det(T^{\det}_{\varepsilon_{5}}(T_{\varepsilon_{3}}(\mathbb{F}_{0})))

\displaystyle=\det(\mathbb{F}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathbb{F}_{0}|\leq 2/\varepsilon_{3}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\det\mathbb{F}_{0}>\varepsilon_{5}\}}+\mathbb{I}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathbb{F}_{0}|>2/\varepsilon_{3}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\det\mathbb{F}_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{5}\}}+\mathbb{I}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathbb{F}_{0}|>2/\varepsilon_{3}\}})>\varepsilon_{5}

the inequality above implies

\displaystyle\|(\det\mathbb{F}(t)-\varepsilon_{5})_{-}\|_{2}=0,\text{ for a.e. }t\in(0,T),

and in consequence
\det\mathbb{F}\geq\varepsilon_{5}
a.e. in
Q_{T}
.

Step 8: convergence with
\varepsilon_{2}\to 0^{+}
.
As in the previous step, due to the energy bounds on the velocity we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{2}(0,T;(W^{1,2}_{\bm{n},\diver}(\Omega))^{*}),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}_{\bm{n},\diver}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<10/3,

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{v},

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

Moreover, as is standard already in this proof, one can deduce that
\{\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{2}}\}_{\varepsilon_{2}>0}
is bounded in
(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3}
. Now, we shall repeat the reasoning given in Step 6 for convergence with
\varepsilon_{4}\to 0^{+}
to augment the convergence of the elastic tensor and temperature. by weak lower semicontinuity of an
L^{2}
norm of
\nabla_{x}(\theta^{1-\lambda/2})
and the established convergence (
3.68
) we can deduce from (
3.57
)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}})^{\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\leq C(\lambda,K,\varepsilon_{3}),\qquad\lambda\in(1,2),

(3.78)

Then, again one can repeat the argumentation under (
2.37
) we can deduce that
\{\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}\}_{\varepsilon_{2}>0}
is bounded in
L^{q}(Q_{T})
for
q<5/3
and
\{\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}\}_{\varepsilon_{2}>0}
is bounded in
L^{q}(Q_{T})
for
q<5/4
. Similarly as in Step 6, we wish to follow the argument in Subsection
2.2.1
, which means that we need the equations for the entropy
\eta^{\varepsilon_{2}}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
. As the functions
1/\theta
and
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
are no longer proper test functions, we may follow the argument about convergence established in Step 6. Similarly as for (
3.59
) we obtain

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon_{2}}+\diver_{x}(\eta^{\varepsilon_{2}}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{2}})-\diver_{x}\left(\kappa\frac{\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}}\right)\\
&=\frac{\kappa|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}})^{2}}+\frac{2\nu|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{2}}|^{2}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}}+\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{2}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{2}}}\frac{g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}})\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{2}}):((\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{2}})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{2}})}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}}+\mu^{\varepsilon_{2}},\end{split}

(3.79)

in the sense of operators in
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
, for
\eta^{\varepsilon_{2}}=\ln\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}-g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}})\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{2}})
. Note that we know that
T^{\varepsilon_{4}}\to 0
from the end of Step 6 and discussion around (
3.68
). Moreover, we have good enough convergence properties in
\varepsilon_{4}
to simply converge from (
3.60
) to obtain

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{2}})+\diver_{x}(\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{2}})\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{2}})\\
&+\tau\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{2}}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{2}}}\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{2}}):((\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{2}})^{2}-\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{2}})=2(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{2}}-\mathbb{I}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{2}}\frac{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}}.\end{split}

(3.80)

in the sense of operators on
C^{1}([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega})
for
\tilde{\psi}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
defined as in (
3.9
). At this point, there are no additional terms as in Step 6, hence we may simply copy the argument in Subsection
2.2.1
to deduce

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}\rightarrow\theta\quad\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T},

which combined with (
3.78
), and our comments below (
3.78
) gives us

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{q}(0,T;(W^{1,q}(\Omega))^{*}),q>5

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<5/3,

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\nabla_{x}\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<5/4,

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T},

\displaystyle(\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}})^{\frac{2-\lambda}{2}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\theta^{\frac{2-\lambda}{2}},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)),\,\lambda\in(0,1).

Moreover, analysing (
3.79
), one can obtain similarly to (
2.45
) and (
2.47
) that

\displaystyle\|\ln\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega))}+\|\ln\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega))}\leq C,

(3.81)

with a constant independent of
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{5},\varepsilon_{6}
. Hence, by the Banach–Alaoglu theorem

\displaystyle\ln\theta^{\varepsilon_{2}}\rightharpoonup\ln\theta,\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)).

When it comes to
\{\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{2}}\}_{\varepsilon_{2}>0}
, one can simply follow the same argumentation as in Step 6 to deduce

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{2}(0,T;((W^{1,2}(\Omega))^{3\times 3})^{*}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }(L^{\infty}(Q_{T}))^{3\times 3},

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<+\infty,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{F},

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

By equation (
3.67
), the sequence of internal energies inherits the regularities and convergence from the temperature and the elastic tensor. We have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\partial_{t}e,

\displaystyle\text{ weakly* in }L^{q}(0,T;(W^{1,q}(\Omega))^{*}),q>5

\displaystyle e^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }L^{q}(Q_{T}),\,q<5/3,

\displaystyle e^{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow e,

\displaystyle\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

And again, to augment the convergence of the velocity gradient, one can repeat the argument given under (
3.47
) in Step 4. All of the convergences are enough to go from (
3.64
)–(
3.66
) to

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}+\diver_{x}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\bm{v}|^{2})\bm{v}\otimes\bm{v})-\diver_{x}\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v})=0,

(3.82)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}+\Diver_{x}(\mathbb{F}\otimes\bm{v})-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\,\mathbb{F}\frac{(\theta-\varepsilon_{6})_{+}}{\theta}+\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}\frac{(\det\mathbb{F}-\varepsilon_{5})_{+}}{\det\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}-\mathbb{F})=0,

(3.83)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e+\diver_{x}(e\bm{v})-\diver_{x}(\kappa(\theta)\nabla_{x}\theta)-\mathbb{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{3},\varepsilon_{6}}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}=0,

(3.84)

where (
3.82
)–(
3.84
) are to be understood in the weak sense in the sense of an operator on a predual space to the time derivative. Note that from (
3.67
) we keep

\begin{split}e(t,x)=\theta(t,x)+(g_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x))-\theta(t,x)g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\theta(t,x)))\tilde{\psi}(\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}),\end{split}

(3.85)

for
\tilde{\psi}
defined as in (
1.21
).

Step 9: convergence with
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{5},\varepsilon_{6}\to 0^{+}
.
The arguments here are analogous to the ones in the previous step. The only reason we have singled out the convergence in
\varepsilon_{2}
, was the possibility to obtain the equality (
3.23
). Hence, we skip the argument here. We only note that by Fatou’s lemma, from (
3.75
) and (
3.81
) we may deduce

\|\ln\theta\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega))},\|\ln\det\mathbb{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega))}<+\infty,

which will imply
\theta>0
,
\det\mathbb{F}>0
, even after we converge with the lower bounds obtained in Step 3 and Step 7 to
0
. Here, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{v}+\diver_{x}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\bm{v}|^{2})\bm{v}\otimes\bm{v})-\diver_{x}\mathbb{T}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v})=0,

(3.86)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{F}+\Diver_{x}(\mathbb{F}\otimes\bm{v})-\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}|)\nabla_{x}\bm{v}\mathbb{F}+\frac{\tau(\theta)}{2}(\mathbb{F}\,\mathbb{F}^{T}\,\mathbb{F}-\mathbb{F})=0,

(3.87)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}e+\diver_{x}(e\bm{v})-\diver_{x}(\kappa(\theta)\nabla_{x}\theta)-\mathbb{T}(\theta,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{D}\bm{v}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}=0,

(3.88)

where (
3.86
)–(
3.88
) are to be understood in the weak sense in the sense of an operator on a predual space to the time derivative. Note that from (
3.85
) we keep

\begin{split}e(t,x)=\theta(t,x)+(g(\theta(t,x))-\theta(t,x)g^{\prime}(\theta(t,x)))\tilde{\psi}(\mathbb{F}\mathbb{F}^{T}),\end{split}

(3.89)

for
\tilde{\psi}
defined as in (
1.21
).

Step 10: convergence with
\varepsilon_{3}\to 0^{+}
.
The last step is in some sense the easiest one, as we can simply copy the arguments given for weak stability of solutions. The only question is whether we have appropriate a priori bounds as in Proposition
2.7
and Corollary
2.8
, where Corollary
2.8
follows from using the equation together with Proposition
2.7
. Note that the only problem that could appear are the a priori bounds related to entropy and lambda entropy, as those are the quantities, in which we cannot keep the equality throughout the approximating sequence. Instead, looking at Step 6 and Step 8, we have

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon_{3}}+\diver_{x}(\eta^{\varepsilon_{3}}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{3}})-\diver_{x}\left(\kappa\frac{\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}}}\right)\\
&=\frac{\kappa|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})^{2}}+\frac{2\nu|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{3}}|^{2}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}}}+\tau\frac{g(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})|\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{3}}-\mathbb{I}|^{2}}{\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}}}+\mu^{\varepsilon_{3}},\end{split}

(3.90)

in the sense of operators in
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
, and

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon_{3}}_{\lambda}-\tau|\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{3}}-\mathbb{I}|^{2}(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})-g^{\prime}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})^{\lambda})\\
&\qquad+2(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{3}}-\mathbb{I}):\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{3}}\Lambda_{\varepsilon_{3}}(|\mathbb{F}^{\varepsilon_{3}}|)(h_{\lambda}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})-g^{\prime}(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})^{\lambda})\\
&=(1-\lambda)\kappa\frac{|\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})^{2-\lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x+2\nu\frac{|\mathbb{D}\bm{v}^{\varepsilon_{3}}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})^{2-\lambda}}+\frac{g(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})|\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon_{3}}-\mathbb{I}|^{2}}{(\theta^{\varepsilon_{3}})^{1-\lambda}}+\nu^{\varepsilon_{3}}_{\lambda},\end{split}

(3.91)

as an operator in
(W^{1,5}(Q_{T}))^{*}
, for
\mu^{\varepsilon_{3}},\nu^{\varepsilon_{3}}_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{M}^{+}(Q_{T})
. But since both equations can be tested by
1
, and the measures are positive, one can proceed in the arguments.
